“We’re both lucky that Charles doesn’t give a damn about anything but drug busts,” Rio murmurs pointedly. “Neither of us need to go to court for a fucking domestic right now.”

“Oh, please, like any of that would hold up in court,” Agatha scoffs sharply. “That was the most illegal arrest I’ve ever seen, heard of, or been a part of, Special Agent.”

Rio glares at her. “Actually, it was quite legal. You physically assaulted a government agent.” Rio pauses, narrowing her eyes as she adds, “twice.”

“Maybe next time don’t lean into a purps car,” she advises with a light shrug. Rio stares at her, lip steadily bleeding. Shifting on her feet, Agatha clears her throat and tosses her hands in the air. Agatha looks at her as if to say, ‘what can you do?’.

“And then you bit me. . .?”

    This House Ain't a Home Anymore The air is heavy with impending rain, gentle mist resting on the shoulders of her coat as Agatha steps out of her car and shuts the door. Being as late in the year as it is, it’s pitch black by only five, and it feels like midnight as she walks up the sidewalk to her home.

    Brick foundation with white side paneling built above it paint a pleasant enough image. The flower beds are thriving – no thanks to her, her perky neighbor simply wouldn’t let her kill these flowers. It’s a picture-perfect house, on the outside.

    Nothing greets her as she steps through the front door. Nobody smiles in glee at her arrival. The house is dark and empty, and there’s a hollowness within it that she’s been ignoring for years, now. Agatha shuts the door behind her, mail in hand, and sighs as she leans back against it.

    Memories of a house filled with laughter and love haunt her every time she steps foot in this precious house, but she can’t bring herself to move. To give it up and forget it all so completely. So, Agatha stayed. For some masochistic reason, she stays.

    Stayed in the house haunted by ghosts of herself and who she once was and could have been. Agatha can still smell it in the –

    There's a thumping on the door behind her, reverberating through her body as she leans against the wood, someone grabbing for her attention.

    With a growl she pushes off from the door and flicks lights on as she walks to the kitchen, slapping the mail on the counter and ripping her coat off. The temptation to grab a glass of wine and curl up on her couch in the effort of forgetting her life is something she wants to succumb to, were it not for knocking throughout her house.

It’s Friday night and Agatha’s biggest plan was about to be to mop her dining room floor while listening to old music from before a time in her life where everything made her angry.

The 'friendly neighbor' automatically digs out Agatha’s wine opener and uncorks one bottle like she’s done this a hundred times, eyes dancing all over Agatha’s kitchen and then her mail.     In response Agatha groans and rolls her eyes, drinking heavily from the glass. She winces and looks down at the dark wine then up at Wanda questioningly. It’s good . Wanda merely shrugs and looks at her expectantly. Perhaps the woman has picked something up from Agatha's own choices all this time.

    “If you still want to be with her, why are you holding back?” Wanda asks kindly.

The words tumble past her lips without thought and she’s spewing half finished sentences to Wanda as if they mean anything and she’s a mess, there are tears gathering in her eyes but her voice is blessedly solid and strong.

    “We were good with him,” she admits, her gaze distant as she stares at the surface of the water.

Agatha had a brief moment of frustration – it had been a long day, she’d been dealing with a horrible mess at work for a week and her Friday was being spent covered in soapy water – but then Nicky had laughed at her and Agatha had laughed with him.

    Rio walked in, then, just getting home from work.

    ‘Hello, lawyer,’ she greeted With a low purr, as always, bending down to kiss Agatha on the cheek.

    Agatha had eyed her and splashed her gently with water. Rio’s eyes had gleamed and before she knew it they were both completely soaked, and it ended with all of them taking a bath that night.

They had put on soft pajamas and curled up together on the couch in front of a fire, and Rio taught Nicky more Spanish, little by little. Agatha chimed in once or twice, correcting his pronunciation, and Rio looked at her.

She took my last moment with him, she shut down and pulled away after he died and she didn’t grieve, Wanda,” Agatha says, finishing her story a while later. “I was torn to pieces and there she was, perfectly fine as if nothing happened. She’s heartless,“ the words feel wrong and sour in her mouth. They don’t feel right, and she doesn’t know why, but she wants to take it back as soon as she’s said it.

    “Of course, I do,” she scoffs. “Always.”

    Agatha didn’t answer Wanda's question. Did you and she said I do. She stares at the foot of the bath, struggling to push those thoughts out of her head. No. No. She hates Rio for what she did.

    It's all she has left. . .she has to hate her.

She couldn’t make herself forget those happy years, wouldn’t let herself forget them, but she moved on. Went back to work the next Monday and told not a soul of her horrible weekend.

She could anticipate Agatha’s reactions and words and it grated on her to no end then and surely now that means she would unravel everything that she's been working for.

    she nods. Agatha leans closer as she sneers, “Then why the hell did you come in here?”

This dominating version of her is doing things to her Agatha would never admit to anyone, she's barely admitting it to herself, and she mutely shakes her head with her lips parted.

    Thank god Rios a good agent and knows how to lie properly.

It's just by law. Last night was a weakness. This is all just for a case, after all, and it's good for her to remember that this is all a charade. One big con and she's always appreciated and loved those. This is no different.

    It shouldn’t hurt.

She barely hears Rio leaving the room and the soft click of the door, but in the silence of Rios absence she does hear the soft crackle of a voice coming from her back pocket.

It dies as she looks at the screen and Agatha tosses it to the bed without finding a charger.

They’re playing a part in a story Agatha doesn't want to be in, not anymore. Part of her wants to stay in this perfect fallacy, and the bigger part of her can’t stand to lie like this. The part of her she's curated over a twenty year career that was made to seek validation she never even really wanted in the first place. Her whole life has been curated to please other people and it's never felt as pressing as it does now, as she's reaping the benefits of twenty years of pretending to be someone she's not.

She would help her at the drop of a hat. If Rio said please, Agatha would punch a priest for her. Well. She might do that just because. Priests give her the willies.

    “Gay heart to heart,” she says to explain their appearance, “We have to have them from time to time or we lose the membership.” Jen looks incredulous but doesn’t push the issue.

    “Alright, fine, do you have any presents?” Jen asks, seemingly already aware of the answer before Agatha gives it. Agatha shakes her head and licks her dry lips, wincing at the taste of ash. “Would you like to get some?”

    “Not particularly, but I’m sure I’ll get the evil eye if I don’t have something, so let’s go.” Agatha stuffs her hands in her pockets and starts off down the sidewalk, ideas already surfacing about what she can get that isn’t too sentimental.

    They wander out of that store into a small curio shop. They all split apart to peruse the shelves, the quiet atmosphere of the room too gentle to be disturbed. The shelves are stocked with random items. Maybe it’s an antique store. It’s hard to peg down. Whatever it is, every surface of the place is packed full with menagerie. There’s a glass ball. Tapestries of geometric patterns, others with famous paintings done into them. Along a wall is a mirror with an ornate gold frame, encircling it gently. Agatha meanders slowly, not exactly looking for anything, but she stops as something catches her attention and holds it.

    Set on a double prong wooden pedestal, small, no longer than her forearm, is a knife. The blade is slightly curved, the hilt and blade seamlessly blending together. Lower on the pedestal and also held up by prongs is the sheath, leather with green accents. It’s admittedly a beautiful blade, and Agatha doesn’t think twice as she buys it and has it wrapped up.

    “Find something good?” Jen asks, meeting her at the checkout. She looks down at the wrapping.

    “Yeah,” she says, somewhat shyly looking down at the wrapping. Agatha shakes her head when the clerk asks about a bag and she takes it, tucking it into her pocket. “Its for Rio," she says, because it makes sense, for this charade, that she would buy her something. That's all this is for; to keep up appearances.

    “That's sweet,” Jen says gently. Agatha smiles tightly and William walks up holding a rather creepy owl statue carved out of wood with eyes too big and yellow. It's nearly half the size of him, and Jen and Agatha eye it with distaste.

    “Absolutely not ,” Agatha says. William pouts lightly and looks down at it.

    “Why not? You don’t even know who it’s for!” William defends quickly.

    “If it’s for me, I’ll burn it,” Agatha says with a shrug, turning to the door.

    “Its for me,” he says glumly. Jen wrinkles her nose and tilts her head.

    “Its creepy,” she agrees with Agatha.

    William sighs and puts it back and they leave the small shop. Agatha pulls her collar tighter around her neck and braces herself from the chill. Jen comes up beside her, pressing their arms together as they walk down the sidewalk, and Agatha feels a little warmer for it.

    Four stores later and the sun has gone down, warm cider in their bellies and steaming up from their cups. Agatha had gotten a fresh one before they left the store, letting it warm her chilled hand as she sips at it.

    They’re walking in front of a bar, lightly packed with people, when William is smiling from their talk and he looks over at her. He and Jen have been discussing youth and something about cartoons, she thinks, but she had largely tuned out of the conversation, content to be a bystander in it instead.

    “Why don’t you and Rio have kids?” He asked, with the innocence of someone who hasn’t experienced loss.

    Agatha stops in her tracks, and Jen’s expression falls. Her chest caves in like a supernova and Agatha feels herself crack open and bleed.

    A presence hovers behind her, and a hand slings around her back. Agatha swallows thickly. She turns and shoves the warm cider into Rio’s hand and walks straight into the bar without a word. She needs space. She needs to get out of this charade that is feeling less and less like playing pretend by the moment because she's gotten Rio a gift and William is asking about them and having a family and she can't breathe. The bar is just an easy excuse to get herself space and once she's inside of it it's all too easy to simply get a drink and lose herself within it.

    The bar is loud with laughter and Christmas cheer and Agatha wants to fall through the floor and cry deep and long in a void where she’ll be untouched by anyone, unwitnessed.

    Walking up to the bar, far from the front of the room, Agatha orders two shots, slings them both back, and then calls for bourbon. The glass is cool in her hand that is still warm from the cider, and Agatha allows herself to take a moment. Just one, and she bows her head between her shoulders and sighs deeply.

    Why don’t you and Rio have kids?

    They should. They would have. Before Nicky had gotten sick, they talked about having another. Agatha wasn’t going to be able to carry him, the pregnancy had ruined her and she had almost died that day, but the fates had pity on her, to let her have him. They would’ve had to adopt for a second child, but they were both willing to weather the storm that would bring.

    Fuck. She hurts.

    Agatha slings back the cheap bourbon and orders another, but this one she nurses carefully as she stands alone at the end of the bar. This is a stupid thing to do, and she hasn’t stopped doing stupid things, lately. One after another. Flirting with Rio. Fighting with her. Seducing her. Hurting her. Coercing her into this whole stupid mess. Coming to Salem in the first place.

    Lifting her head, Agatha rubs her knuckle under her jaw and sighs. It took so long for her to put herself back together after Nicky, and this trip has done everything possible to tear her apart. Doing so with ease, as if she’d stitched herself up with floss instead of sealing the cracks of her pain with Molten gold.

    “You haven’t eaten today. . . have you?” Rio asks beside her, already knowing the answer. Agatha had expected her presence, and she's glad, now, to be proven right, because only she could understand what Agatha is currently going through; all of the stages of grief at once. Agatha scoffs and hangs her head, looking down at her drink. “You should slow down.” Her hand rests at Agatha’s lower back.

    Why not let the stupid decisions continue? At this point, she doesn’t care. Agatha leans into Rio and sighs heavily like she’s wanted to this whole trip. She indulges in herself and Rio lets her, wrapping her arms around her and lightly running her fingers through her hair. Agatha shifts, standing up straight and moving her head to rest against Rio’s shoulder, her head nestled under her jaw.

    “I heard what he said,” Rio says quietly. Agatha doesn’t respond, staring up at the bar as someone in a skimpy Santa outfit starts dancing on top of it.

    Leaning into Rios arms as the woman plays with her hair, Agatha feels the cracks of herself crumbling further apart. This should feel good, she wants it to, to be in Rios arms and accept the comfort she should have five years ago. Agatha wants to accept her help, her care, but even now, broken down as she is, she doesn’t think she can. She’ll hollow out if she faces the rejection of Rio Vidal, she needs to stop this before she can't.

    “Let’s get out of here, I can’t think it’s so loud," Rio says into her ear.

    Strobe lights come on as the house lights dim entirely. Some Christmas song that’s been remixed to hell blasts over the speakers. Rio’s face is cast in the green and red lights, flashing in her eyes and it hurts. It all hurts.

    It reminds her too much of a long night ending at a bar with a slow dance and -

    Agatha doesn’t reply, she turns into Rio so they’re hugging and Agatha struggles to hold in the quiet whimper of loss. Rio holds her tight, burying her face in her neck and breathing in deep. It feels like coming home and the thought does make Agatha cry, silent tears tracking down her cheeks. Standing there in her arms, Agatha feels herself starting to fall apart. To accept the path she’s on. It will lead to her ruin, she’s certain of it, but she doesn’t want to leave, can’t find it in herself to turn away.

    “Have you been smoking?” Rio suddenly asks in surprise.

    Leaning away, Agatha cackles and Rio is watching her in amusement as she does. Wiping at her eyes, Agatha relishes the way Rio still has her arms around her waist, her fingers locked together behind her back. She appreciates that Rio knows her so well to know that she can’t pull herself out. The question might be genuine, but Rio did what Agatha couldn’t, and she appreciates it as much as she wishes she wouldn’t have. It had pulled her from her depressing spiral of thoughts, distracting her away from it just enough.

    “Bummed one off of William,” she says, her voice a little hoarse. Rio nods and looks at her too deeply for Agatha’s comfort. “He has a bad habit, apparently.”

    Rio's eyes glint and shine in the multi-color lights. Her hand rubs up and down on her side and Agatha sighs, nuzzling into her. She breathes in Rio's scent and let's it trickle over the cracks in her soul. Agatha thinks back on hard days and coming home to Rio. If Rio was ever home before Agatha, she would smell of fresh dirt and clean air of the garden, and if she was home after Agatha, she smelled like old coffee and papers. Underneath it all had been Rio. Her scent that stayed with her no matter what. It was hard to put a name to, but back then Agatha always thought of it as home.

    “Come on, pay your tab,” Rio beckons after a few moments of quietly allowing Agatha to press against her.

    Not wanting to argue that she’d rather stay here and lose herself in amber for several hours, Agatha simply turns in her arms and waves down the bartender. Rio stays wrapped around her, perching her chin on Agatha’s shoulder as her arms are around her waist. Even as Agatha leans over to sign the receipt and finish her drink, Rio stays until Agatha taps her hands and then they leave the bar.

    At some point the dancers on the bar tear off their clothes and Agatha and Rio look at each other start laughing at the absurdity of two lesbians in a bar at Christmas, present for a strip tease by slutty Santas.

    Someone leans close to them and then cheers, and Agatha and Rio look around to find most eyes on them. One of the Santa’s has something in his hand, reaching out for them. Agatha frowns, unable to see it in the dim light, but then Rio stiffens and Agatha sees what it is in the strobe of the lights. The santa holding it is cheering, and he moves the mistletoe over their heads, looking around at the bar to encourage them. He waves his free arm in the air and the crowd starts chanting for them to kiss.

    “We don’t have to,” Rio offers quietly. Her expression is careful and hesitant, giving Agatha the option to just nod and leave the bar.

    “It’s just a kiss, Vidal.” Agatha turns and grabs her by the shirt, kissing her with everything she’s got.

    (Okay, so yeah, she didn’t eat all day and she had just slammed back two shots and two glasses of bourbon, good decisions really aren’t her forte right now and Rio looks way too kissable)

    It isn’t just a kiss. It never is between them. Agatha moves to hold the side of Rio’s neck and her cheek with her other hand. Rio has her waist in one hand and her wrist in her other, keeping the hand at her cheek in place as they push into each others space.

    If being wrapped in Rio’s arms had felt like home, then kissing her felt like opening the door of her soul and basking in her.

    Agatha feels the cracks and chips in Rio, and wants to spread herself out and fill those cracks with herself. To intertwine so intricately that neither of them can come unraveled. Being kissed by Rio feels a lot like she’s trying to do the same in return, and when her sharp tongue flicks out against her bottom lip, Agatha is all too eager to let her in. Bolts of desire wrapped up in sweltering heat strike through her, plunging down to her core. Agatha steps impossibly closer to her and curls her fingers into the back of her neck, sucking on Rio’s lip for only a moment until the cheers and whoops and hollers of the crowd reach her ears.

    Smiling at her, Agatha pulls away, satisfied at the dazed look in Rio’s eyes. The woman has a lazy grin, kiss swollen lips, and hooded eyes. Job well done, Agatha looks up at the Santa that had been holding the mistletoe and offers him an arrogant smile as she grabs Rio by the shoulders and steers her out of the bar.

    She's still smiling as they leave the bar, the chill of the air immediately sinking into her bones, more so now that Rio isn’t wrapped around her.

    Agatha crosses her arms and looks around at the emptied sidewalks and vacant streets. Rio breathes in deep and whistles as she breathes out, and Agatha looks over to see her shaking her head, reaching into her pocket.

    “Car’s a few blocks down, here.” Rio hands her something as she starts walking and Agatha watches her take a few steps, amused that she’s pretending to recover quickly.

    Agatha knows that the jilt in Rio’s step is from something she certainly can’t recover from quickly – and not without a helping hand – but she looks down in her hands in favor of watching her walk. A crumpled carton of cigarettes and a lighter sit in her palm. Scoffing, Agatha pulls one out and shakes her head as she catches up to Rio who’s waiting at a crosswalk.

    Agatha has two in her mouth by the time she reaches, and she lights them both before handing one to Rio.

    The agent eyes her in amusement and takes it.

    “I thought you didn’t smoke,” Agatha hedges. Rio shrugs and holds her gaze, admitting something in them.

    “Picked it up in the last few years,” she says casually, setting it between her lips and dragging from it.

    There’s a reason Agatha had avoided smoking, and it seems to be the same reason Rio picked it up. They had both subconsciously associated the smell, the act, whatever it is, with an easier time. Back when Agatha was still in school and Rio had a new, shitty job at the local station and they would stumble home smelling of smoke and beer.

    “Funny,” Agatha murmurs, though she isn’t laughing as she stares back, admitting her own truth with her eyes, “I dropped it.”

    She turns away, regret clawing at her for ruining the moment. Agatha crosses the street slowly, blinking hard as her world shifts under her feet.

    “Easy, your honor,” Rio croons, her voice distorted from the cigarette hanging between her lips as she steadies Agatha with an arm around her shoulder. “Wouldn’t want you to get a concussion.”

    Agatha laughs for some reason, and Rio scoffs out a laugh with her.

    “Your honor," she drawls provocatively, "ooh, talk dirty to me,” Agatha purrs without thinking, looking over at Rio with a grin.

    “Going to sentence me to community work, your honor?” Rio continues, and why the fuck does that make Agatha horny?

    “Maybe,” Agatha considers. Her eyes flick down to Rio’s lips and she hums, thinking of the taste of her. “I have a few ideas as to how you can pay it off.”

    This strange script they’ve put themselves in would normally make her cringe so deep she’d earn new lines, but with the buzz of the bourbon in her it spurns her on. It makes her warm and energizes her more than it should. Rio hums and drags from her cigarette and Agatha realizes she dropped hers. She looks down at her empty hand, rubbing her fingers together. Oh, well, probably for the best.

    “Here,” Rio murmurs, glancing ahead as she shifts hers to the hand around Agatha's shoulder. Agatha raises her eyebrows but turns her head and drags from it as Rio digs into her pockets. “I filled up your tank, by the way, your car gets horrible gas mileage.”

    “I don’t drive it because it’s good for the environment,” Agatha drawls with a small laugh. Her voice has become a little hoarse from the smoke, and she nearly purrs at the way Rio’s has, too.

    “Naughty, naughty,” Rio tsk’s, shaking her head as she pulls out the keys. “You know, your honor, you really should care more about the ozone layer.”

    “It shouldn’t be so sexy to hear you call me that,” Agatha admits freely. Rio raises her eyebrows and smiles.

    “Hmm, but it is,” she says. Rio looks ahead of them, hesitates, and then leans in. “You can call me Special Agent in return if that will make you feel better.”

    “And will you like it?” Agatha asks, delighted at how easy it is to flirt with her. Rio shrugs and her eyes glimmer.

    “Find out for yourself,” she says roughly.

    Agatha swallows, and oh that is quite the temptation, isn’t it?

    “Maybe I will.” She keeps her voice light and careful, because even in her drunken haze she can sense this should be handled with the utmost care.

    Rio nods and raises her other hand to point down the sidewalk.

    “Car’s just ahead.” She takes her arm back to pull from her cigarette and then drop it on the ground, stepping on it to put it out. Rio bends down and swipes it up, rolling it between her fingers and watching ash fall out. When they pass a cigarette disposal in front of another bar, she tosses it inside. Agatha watches her in silence, noting the flex of the tendons in her hand and wrist, admiring the gentle bulge of the veins laced under her skin.

    “Don’t get sick,” Rio warns with a grin as they approach the car at the end of the block, unaware of Agatha's thoughts.

    “I wasn’t the one throwing up on the sidewalk last time,” Agatha retorts immediately. “I am not a bad driver, and you know it.”

    “Liar. You took a corner at thirty-five, anyone who isn’t a race car driver would’ve thrown up,” Rio teases. She opens the door for Agatha and shuts it for her, rounding the front of the car to slide in. “Maybe we should get you on the track, so you can get it out of your system and be a law abiding driver on the streets.”

    “Please,” Agatha scoffs, getting comfortable in her seat. “I’ll just go on the interstate when the cops aren’t there, same thing.”

    “Sounds like a confession to an officer of the law,” Rio teases easily.

    Agatha laughs and puts her seatbelt on as Rio starts driving.

    “Drive, Vidal, I want to see ten and two on that wheel, unless you’re all talk.” Agatha’s cheeks hurt, from smiling, she realizes.

    It hurts, abruptly and without end, to realize that as she looks over at Rio who is also smiling so easy, that Agatha is still maddeningly in love with her.

    Agatha briefly considers throwing herself out of the car.

    “Come on, you need water and food,” Rio says, leaving the car now that they're parked in the mayoral driveway.

    Agatha groans as she slinks down into the seat. Her stomach tumbles and turns, and when Rio opens the door she immediately steps back as Agatha leans out and gets sick. Rio says something quietly in Spanish and Agatha can’t focus enough to hear it, she just feels Rio brush her hair behind her ear. Rio leans into the car and unbuckles her, leading her out of the seat.

    “And maybe brush your teeth,” Rio murmurs.

    Agatha sighs as she leans into Rio’s side and climbs the stairs. The house is completely dark as they walk up to it, and Agatha has no idea what time it is. She feels significantly better as they enter the dark house and Rio takes her right to the kitchen. It’s completely quiet inside and for that Agatha is grateful.

    “I think there’s something in here that won’t be too horrible to eat,” Rio says. She tugs one of the barstools out from under the kitchen island and then deposits Agatha on it before turning to the fridge.

    Her head is spinning, but not from alcohol, from that she's recovered. No, it’s spinning with the realizations in her mind and heart, and the bright, burning hope that she has a chance. Agatha is embraced by the warmth of their encounters tonight, and she watches Rio dig around in the fridge with that warmth nearly sweltering her. They're still good together, she thinks, maybe. . . .maybe there's a chance that Agatha could forgive her. She certainly won't ever forget what's happened, but maybe they could move on. Hating Rio is exhausting when faced with her, Agatha, for some reason, decides she'd instead like to. . . maybe she could. . . It brings a new question to the front of her mind, and Agatha doesn't think before opening her mouth.

    “Why don’t you hate me?” Agatha asks.

    Everything flips on a dime with her words. The easy, carefree way they had flirted and kissed and touched is all gone. The air is tense and shaky, and fresh tears pull up to Agatha’s eyes. She’s thinking of William’s question and she can see that Rio is, too. Rio stops with the fridge open and stands up straight. She closes it, contemplating Agatha’s questions quietly. Rio smiles sadly and her lips twitch with the action, as though she didn’t mean to do it but doesn’t want to take it back. Sighing, she looks down and away, and shifts her shoulders, faces Agatha head on.

    “I could never hate you, Agatha,” she says calmly. “It was all you. That’s always been our problem, we were never on the same page.” Rio rolls her bottom lip into her mouth and taps her nails on the counter, looking Agatha over as she holds herself back from crying and releases her lip. “I don’t think we ever have been.”

    Same page. . .no. No, they haven’t been.

    Despite being the one to start this conversation – forcing it down Rio’s throat, poking and prodding at old wounds that never healed properly – Agatha scrambles to take it all back. To think through and desperately she tries to find some moment in time that they both knew what they wanted and it was the same thing.

    Agatha thinks through years and years spent together living as one. There’s the job Rio took that kept her away from home – their breaking point – and then before that was Agatha panicking and isolating herself. Nicky got sick and it was Agatha alone in the hospital room, and she remembers – distantly, she has tried to force this memory from her mind but it rises up ugly and clear, now – forcing Rio out. Away from her. From him.

    ‘Don’t try to take it back now, it’s too late, ’ Agatha had sneered at her, tears swelling her eyes and making her throat raw.

    The hospital smelled like Death, and after that, Agatha could only smell that on Rio. She always smelled like that hospital room and Agatha never figured out how, didn’t want to think about it until now as she stares at Rio and something slots into place

    They’ve always been on the same page.

    Agatha in that hospital room, and when she was gone, Rio slipped in, making sure Nicky was never alone. Avoiding Agatha’s raw anger. It meant that Rio never saw him awake, probably. Rio stepped back to let Agatha grieve in her way, all the while. . . Agatha had always assumed that Rio was off somewhere else, doing whatever secret thing she had started, she never stopped to think about why Rio was never home at night.

    More memories are rushing in, and Agatha is staggered under the weight of them.

    Agatha forcing Rio away from her time and time again. Ignoring her out of spite for the way Rio had abandoned them. As far as Agatha knew, Rio practically had, she was always gone with no explanation and the few times they did see each other they were fighting. It, eventually, led to Agatha kicking Rio out of their house, forcing her to find a home elsewhere as they sought the help of a counselor. Two sessions were all they had managed, and then they never saw each other again. Not until two nights ago at a stupid restaurant in the town she grew up in.

    Another time, of waking up in the middle of the night, when Nicky was young, before he was sick. Rio would come home late, taking double shifts here and there, and she would sleep straight on the couch, either too tired to walk all the way to their bedroom or not wanting to wake her, Agatha never asked. Nicky was sobbing and screaming from a nightmare and Agatha wasn’t even awake when she started sprinting down the hall towards his room.

    Rio and Agatha slammed into each other in the hallway and neither of them stopped, they grabbed each other into an upright position and kept running. They moved in perfect sync, where one of them left a gap the other filled it.

    Agatha picked Nicky up and cradled him to her chest as Rio wrapped a blanket around him and rubbed his back. Rio murmured soft lullabies to him – the sound soothed all three of them, the soft, familiar Spanish easing their fears - and Agatha bounced him up and down.

    Eventually, he fell asleep, but it took at least an hour.

    After that, Agatha took him to bed, and Rio circled her arms around the both of them, Agatha leaning into her chest. They fell asleep like that, all bundled up as one family and Rio still had her clothes on from work, but none of them cared.

    There are more memories that pass before Agatha’s eyes like misted over film recaps. Frayed and burnt at the edges, but there at the center is a clarity coming into more and more focus. The roll of film passes before her eyes and hesitates on that image of Rios distraught face as Agatha shoves her out of their house, that moment right before Rio hid her emotions like she’s always done so well.

    It burns Agatha’s eyes, brings her back to them, now; standing in the dark kitchen of her mother’s house, regret is an acrid scent in the air.

    “I’ll leave you to it,” Rio says, tilting her chin up nonchalantly. That mask is in place, and Agatha can see the redness of her eyes, she can’t hide that even as expert as she is at hiding her emotions.

    They're both raw and freshly hurting. William’s question echoes in her head, and she knows Rio heard it and felt that pain, too. Knows it struck them the same way and dug in deep, ripping them both to shreds.

    Agatha’s fingers twitch at her sides and she steps after her. Rio looks down and it’s in slow motion that she walks away, and Agatha feels a pull, an ache in her chest that she’s never felt before. Never let herself feel like she is now, allowing the feeling to overwhelm and swell within her. Her mouth opens and closes a few times, and Rio is at the archway of the kitchen, now. A few more steps and she’ll be in the hallway, too close to the front door. To leaving forever.

    There is not another chance for this. She's thinking of that lullaby that had always worked on Nicky, and the sound of Rio's voice lilting the melodies is echoing in Agatha's head as she sees her last opportunity for this about to leave forever. The faint, simmering hope in her chest urges her to react without thought for the umpteenth time tonight.

    “Señora Santana ,” Agatha sings softly, her voice shaking with unshed tears. Rio stops in place and raises her head, hands in her pockets. Agatha swallows the knot in her throat and keeps going, her voice wavering and breaking on every single word. “¿Por qué llora El niño?”

    Rio stands perfectly still, staring up at the ceiling with her eyes closed, from what Agatha can see of her. Agatha rakes in a shaking breath and pushes through the rest of the lullaby, her voice becoming quieter and quieter until: “ Ya no llores niño. Aquí tengo dos. Una pa’ la Virgen. Y otra para ti ,” she finishes with a final waver of her voice, breaking off to cover her mouth with her hand as she chokes down a sob.

    Rio's shoulders lift and lower as she breathes in deep, her whole body stiff.

    “What’s the point of that? You hate me that much that you –“ Rios voice is firm, but there’s a rawness to it as she stares down the hall towards the front door, that lets Agatha know her eyes are thick with tears.

    “I hated you,” Agatha says, the Spanish rolling off her tongue with a frightening ease despite not having spoken it in years. Not since living with Rio. “For so long,” she continues, taking another step forward, trailing her hand along the counter to keep herself steady.

    “Nice speech, Agatha,” Rio sharply says, looking almost over her shoulder, but her profile is barred in shadow and Agatha can’t make her face out well. Rio replies to her in English, and Agatha sees her eyes glisten.

    Agatha's mind doesn’t communicate those thoughts to her mouth.

    “And that anger kept me going,” Agatha presses her hand over her heart, hating the way it breaks at being denied something that was theirs, to Agatha at least. She learned Spanish for Rio, then they taught it to Nicky. To Agatha, the language had been theirs . Something for just their little family. So to speak it to Rio and be denied the words in return makes her ache so deeply she’d rather carve out her own heart. Her fingers curl over her shirt as if she could dig in deep and yank it out, to offer it to Rio as sacrifice

    “It was easier, to hate you, than to admit that there was nothing I could do for him,” Agatha continues in Spanish and Rio scoffs and shakes her head as she turns to look at her. Rio storms up to her, silent tears down her cheeks as she rips one hand from her coat pocket and points at Agatha, digging that finger into Agatha’s chest.

    “If I let go of that anger I was going to fall apart, and I was afraid of who I would be if I was put back together,” she says quickly.

    “I was afraid I wouldn’t be enough for you. That I wouldn’t be able to put myself back together the right way. That you would. . .” Agatha’s eyes flood with tears and she pauses to swallow them before continuing. “That you would leave, because of that. It was. . .it felt easier to make you hate me. To make you leave and to be in control of that.”

    “And now?” Rio asks, her eyes sharp and filled with acid. “So, what? You want me to – “

    “I don’t want anything,” she interrupts. Agatha smiles sadly and takes her hands; cupping Rios face and she feels her lip wobble with the way Rio immediately softens and relaxes.

That perfect mask is finally gone and Agatha has never felt pain like this. “In every way. I would do anything for you, Agatha, always, and you knew that so well, didn’t you?” Rio looks like she wants to offer a smile but she quells it and breathes in deep, pulling away. “You always kept yourself so guarded from everyone around you, maybe it’s my fault for thinking I was immune.”

    “Don’t ,” Rio snaps. “Don’t come after me, Agatha. You owe me that, after all you’ve done.”

    Fluidly, she turns on her heel and walks away. Rio disappears down the hallway in quiet steps, her long coat fluttering behind her – Agatha had gotten her that coat, one winter, hadn't she? Is she grasping for straws now? Desperate for any kind of connection between them? – and the house is dark and quiet. The kitchen that has just been warmly filled with their wandering memories when they arrived has now been cast in cold, unforgiving Shadow and Agatha is the darkness that leeches the light.

    The front door opens.

    This can’t be happening. Rio had weathered every storm. She withstood all of Agatha’s tirades, every single one this cannot. She isn’t possibly – why now? What changed? What has she done?

    What is she doing?

    Take Me Back Chapter Summary Agatha takes three steps forward and eight steps back. Or does Rio?

    Rio tightens her arm around Agatha as she sets her coffee mug down. From the side of her eyes, Agatha sees that Rio is staring straight ahead, her expression dangerous in it’s own way, and she shakes her head imperceptibly. Agatha frowns and rolls her tongue over her teeth, then twists her head to look at Jen. Jen simply seems confused, and she looks between Evanora and Agatha with mild concern. William's eyes are bouncing between all of them, his brows pulling together.

    “The study,” Evanora says. She doesn’t wait for her, she turns and walks away.

    Agatha’s stomach sinks and she nonchalantly takes a drink of her coffee and then sets it down. She pulls out of Rio’s embrace but lightly smacks her ass as she passes behind her. For good luck, and Rio manages to smirk in amusement down at her coffee as Agatha glances behind her.

    They both are recalling the same thing, Agatha is certain, but she doesn’t let herself dwell on it, today. If she is to face down Evanora Harkness alone, she can’t have her head – metaphorically – between Rios thighs. She would rather be there, though, that much is certain, and the thought makes her clear her throat tightly.

    “Warm the car, will you, dear?” Agatha trills over her shoulder sarcastically, sauntering down the hallway as if she doesn’t feel dread gnawing at her, threatening to devour her from the inside out.

    As soon as she’s out of sight of the kitchen, Agatha allows herself one moment. It’s something of a ritual of hers, if one had to put a name to it. Before doing whatever it is she dreads, she allows herself all of one second to react the way she wants – within reason, considering her surroundings. Her shoulders drop, her gait slows, she hangs her head slightly. Agatha lets out a quiet sigh and in that sigh releases the anxieties and hurt in her chest. Two steps later she’s holding her head high, guarding herself so securely from whatever may come as she turns the next corner into the study. Agatha shimmies her shoulders ever so slightly and enters the study.

    Evanora is standing by the window, waiting for her, staring out at the snowy backyard so her back is to Agatha. It's intentional, she's sure of that.

    “Close the door,” she says.

    “Don’t want them to hear it when you plot to lynch me?” Agatha drawls.

    Evanora doesn’t reply to that, so Agatha shuts the door behind her. It’s a set of glass french double doors. Shutting it barely does anything as far as blocking out the sound, she knows from experience, but maybe it’s just the power play of telling Agatha what to do. It’s hard to gauge things like that with Evanora.

    “You will not corrupt that boy,” Evanora says, facing her. Her eyes are sharp and dark with a threat of danger. Agatha scoffs and rolls her eyes. She crosses one arm over her stomach and rests the back of her elbow on the back of her hand, holding her hand aloft in disinterest. Agatha makes a point to look around at the office and not at Evanora.

    This is her office for show, the one she brings politicians in to in order to win them over or bribe them, or even scare the law, as far as Agatha knows. The dark oak desk in the center of the room is imposing all on its own. No personal knick-knacks adorn its surface, and there is a lamp and a set of rather expensive pens resting on top of it. Dark bookshelves line either side of the room, bracketing the window and reaching all the way up to the ceiling. Encyclopedias and books about politics line the shelves. It's a rather boring spread, if she's honest.

    “I didn’t do a thing, and he’s a big boy, he can make his own decisions,” she replies. “I wonder what people would say if they knew who you really are," Agatha says, widening her eyes and then resuming her flat, disinterested expression. "You can donate to the charity, but that doesn’t mean you aren’t the reason it’s there in the first place.” Agatha purses her lips and shrugs as she glances at Evanora and then back to the bookshelves.

    “Stop with the lies, Agatha,” Evanora thunders. Agatha actually jumps at the volume of her voice, and she looks at her with wide, unsure eyes as Evanora faces her. “I tolerate you and your. . .Rio under my roof, because I have no choice," Evanora sneers. Then she shifts, her broad shoulders are imposing when backlit by the snowwy backyard beyond her, and the shadows crossing her face make Agatha feel two feet tall. "I will not tolerate your kind doubling in size the longer you are here.”

    Agatha’s mouth is hanging open in shock, and she scoffs to herself, lost for words.

    “Is that why you couldn’t stand the sight of your grandson? You didn’t want to admit that you cared for someone who came from such stock?” Agatha says, the words taste vile in her mouth.

    Agatha hates herself for her own words. How dare she bring Nicky into this? This precious boy who loved and laughed and cared. Agatha hates herself for doing so, but the look on Evanora’s face makes her feel even more regret about it than before. She knows that look, and it’s always preceding something that makes Agatha feel like a little girl forced out of her home too young. She tries to guard her heart from the incoming words, to wrap steel around herself and let whatever comes next ricochet off of her.

    “That boy was better off without you.”

    Agatha sees red.

    It is a good thing Agatha has stayed by the door and not taken a single step further into the room. She has nothing around her to reach for and throw, and oh, how she wants to. Agatha wants to scream and rage and ruin, more than anything else she wants to do something that will ensure she never sees her mothers expression ever again. But Agatha has played the long game too many times before, she knows she needs to bide her time and wait. To be smart about this, and she doesn’t want to, but she forces herself to swallow her explosive actions, tears silently welling in her eyes in the wake of her anger.

    “The same as dad was better without you?” Agatha volleys back with a light shrug of her shoulder.

    Evanora’s expression doesn’t change, and she hadn’t expected it to.

    Breathing in deep, Agatha sniffs casually, as if the words thrown around the air between them don't make her want to fall to her knees and cry, and reaches back for the door, turning the handle and leaning back into it.

    “Always so great to have these talks, mom,” Agatha says, sickly sweet. “I don’t know where I’d be without them.” She smiles, for good measure, and leaves.

    The hallway is silent and empty as she keeps her hand on the doorknob, pausing for a moment. to her right is the soft murmur of conversation, paired with quiet, easy laughter. Behind her is the soft rustle of movement in the office, and within her is the raw emptiness that begs to drag her back into the depths of a rough night she left behind a long time ago. Back to a failed effort to drown her memories in amber liquid.

    Agatha turns away from the kitchen and the soft murmur of voices to the bathroom down the hall.

    Behind the closed door she chokes back a sob and wipes carefully at her eyes, willing the tears not to fall. Fuck. Fuck, every time. Evanora manages to get under the chinks in Agatha’s armor that she thought she’d fixed a long time ago. She’s glad she hasn’t come back here in so long. If she would have come every year like she was invited to, Agatha is positive she would’ve been the shell of who she is today and who she once was. She’s certainly glad she only brought Nicky here once, and never again.

    Making her disappearance quick, Agatha fixes her hair and red eyes with a splash of water, checking herself over in the mirror she leaves the bathroom quickly. She glides down the hall and fits herself back into the kitchen as if nothing happened, leaning against the counter near her left behind coffee mug. Rio is on her right, and her attention is heavy on the side of her face.

    “ - the tickets were really cheap!” William exclaims, some part of a story she hasn’t caught.

    Agatha grabs her coffee mug and sips at it gingerly, wincing at the fact that it isn’t steaming anymore. She swallows it down anyway, anything to get the taste of bitter abandonment from her mouth. Rio’s arm slides around her, and the warmth of her body presses into her side next, her stomach against her waist and her chest against her arm. Agatha releases a sigh of relief she hadn’t meant to, and looks over her shoulder at her, flatly smiling her thanks. With her eyes, Rio asks if she’s okay, and Agatha merely looks away.

    She looks right at Evanora who is walking in the dark of the hallway, watching them with a threat in her eyes.

    The expression is gone by the time Rio looks over at her, and Agatha can feel how much it pains Rio to not glare at her and instead she offers a pleasant smile. Rio’s hand is possessively tight around Agatha’s hip near her waistband, and Agatha leans her weight against Rio in warning.

    “I’ll be at the ballroom all day, preparing for tonight,” Evanora says, her voice as pleasant as it can sound, as she strides into the kitchen as if nothing is wrong. “Your parents will be there, too.” She glances at William so briefly it can barely be considered a glance at all, and he doesn’t seem to give a shit.

    “I’ll stop by to help them. Jen?” He looks up at her and Jen puts her tea down, nodding with a gentle smile.

    “Yeah, we’ll both go.” Her reassurance makes his shoulders relax ever so slightly, and Agatha feels relief that they at least, have each other. Jen looks over at them, her eyes flicking between them and then landing on Agatha with a question she can’t read. “And you two?”

    “More running around in town,” Rio says easily, before Agatha can reply.

    “We need to check on our reservation.”

    That gives Agatha pause and she looks back at Rio curiously, but the woman doesn’t look back at her.

    “Right,” she says, deciding to just go with it. “If we get done in time, we’ll stop by before it starts.” It’s a lie, and Jen knows it, but understands, thankfully, nodding at her without any dissatisfaction in her eyes. “Are you coming back to the house after setting up?” Agatha downs the rest of her coffee as Jen shakes her head, answering for all of them.

    “No, we’re changing there.”

    “It starts at nine, I expect you to be there,” Evanora says, her voice somewhat bright and playfully firm. Agatha wants to roll her eyes and mime throwing up, but refrains.

    “Times up,” Rio murmurs in her ear – her lips brush her earlobe and Agatha shivers as she thinks of how those teeth have dug into that tender flesh and Rio had moaned so feminine and needy, so opposite of her normal tone - so nobody else can hear, patting her hip.

    The warning is new, Agatha notes – there are so many new things with Rio, and Agatha's heart clenches for having missed them all, but now that means she can discover her, explore her like new. Despite the fact that they’ve never used this code together, Agatha understands her words perfectly; Rios ran out of patience and if she stays a minute longer she’ll blow this out of the water too early. It prompts memories of Rio ranting about how much she hates Evanora the first and last time they came up to Salem together.

    “Love to stay and chat, but we need to go,” Agatha says loudly. She grabs hers and Rio’s mugs, rinsing them in the sink and then walking around Rio, snagging her hand and pulling her away from the counter. “We’ll be there, don’t worry. Ta!” She waves a hand nonchalantly over her shoulder as they go, not waiting to listen for any echoes of goodbye.

    Agatha leads Rio down the hall and the hand in hers squeezes her tightly, holding on and offering reassurance. It almost makes her cry again, but Agatha is tired of crying.

    She’s also tired of waiting, something has to give.

    Agatha grabs a coat from the rack by the door and then slings her own over her arm. Rio is holding the door open for her, her expression thunderous. Having some self-preservation, for once, Agatha doesn’t make any snide quips as she walks through it and then follows Rio to the car where she opens the door for her before walking around to the driver’s seat.

    Putting her seatbelt on, Agatha pulls up short as she notices Rio facing her. Instead of getting comfortable in her seat, she stays perfectly upright and cautiously meets her eyes.

    “What?” She asks skeptically.

    “What was it this time?” Rio asks sharply, and Agatha doesn’t understand. What was it. . . did she do something wrong? Had she misstepped? “What bullshit did she try to get you with now?”

    Oh. It’s concern that’s drawing her features down. It’s protective anger lighting flames in her eyes.

    Agatha’s heart cracks, subtly, and the gaping maw where it used to sit big and full opens wider as her heart crumbles a little more. It crumbles because of what she’s done to this wonderful, caring woman. How she forced her out of her life in anger and rage and never looked back. How could Rio still care for her? After the last five years?

    After last night?

    “Uh,” Agatha flounders. “Uh, it was about William. She thinks I’m. .

    .corrupting him or something.” She waves her hand and scoffs under her breath, pulling her façade back together as quickly as she can.

    "Corrupting him?" Rio repeats through clenched teeth.

    Rio snarls under her breath and starts the car, taking off as a calm, normal person would. The gear shift snaps as she puts the car into gear and it creaks as Rio holds onto it tightly.

    "Mother dearest doesn’t accept gay people well, you already knew that,”

    Agatha murmurs, watching her curiously.

    Why is this infuriating her so much?

    “Why is this bothering you?” Agatha voices her confusion, watching Rio flex her jaw over and over and readjust her grip on the steering wheel.

    It takes Rio a few moments to respond. She’s pressing her tongue to her cheek in thought, working her jaw side to side. Rio shifts in her seat, her hips jerking up as she settles back down. It’s in anger, Agatha is well aware, but her mind still flies to other strains of thought, desperate to cling to any thoughts that aren't about her mother and the trauma she has because of her.

    “Evanora Harkness is a stain on this earth,” Rio says at last.

    Her eyes are scanning the road ahead meticulously, and she recovers from the car fishtailing carefully, slowing down a little on an icy patch. Agatha doesn’t even look at the road as the car shifts, she blindly grabs the door handle lightly but keeps her eyes on Rio, unworried and trusting in her capabilities to drive safely.

    “People like her love nothing more than to watch people squirm under their thumb, and they always end up with power they shouldn’t have. They take love and twist it into something vile, try to control everything and everyone around them. They deserve to burn for it.”

    The vehemence in her words has Agatha off-kilter. She struggles to find her center again, reaches blindly for her air of nonchalant indifference. It probably doesn't mean anything, she tells herself. Anyone would be upset about such blatant homophobia. That's all this is. She won't let herself be rejected and thrown out again, and certainly not by Rio. That would ruin her, she's certain of it.

    “Yeah, I get that,” she says slowly. “But you. . . “ Agatha trails off.

    “What?” Rio asks defensively, sparing a glance at her. “I’m not allowed to care anymore, Agatha?”

    The words cut deeply through her guard, and Agatha can’t look at her anymore. The question makes it harder for her to hold up that protective wall around herself, and those simple words crack the walls around her.

    “Do whatever you want,” she says dismissively.

    Rio growls under her breath and shifts in her seat again. Agatha curls her knuckles under her jaw as she stares out the window, and Rio takes a deep breath and then lets it out slowly, her grip relaxing on the wheel and the gear shift both.

    The hand gently taking hers almost makes her jump, and Agatha looks over as Rio holds her hand carefully, eyes focused fully between the road and the rearview mirror, but her expression has softened significantly.

    “I still care, Agatha, that never changed,” Rio says quietly. Agatha is already forming a defensive, guarded comment, but Rio doesn’t let her, squeezing her hand, she talks first. “I know how she affects you, and I. . .I can’t stand it. That I can’t do anything for you.” Rio looks over at her, and it really sends the words straight past her defenses.

    “You are certainly under no obligation to,” Agatha says softly.

    She wanted to thank her, to tell her that it meant a lot for Rio to admit that, to care. Agatha won’t let herself be vulnerable, even when she wants to be and it makes her feel guilt, bone deep. Agatha pulls her hand from Rio’s and looks out the window again.

    Rio is watching her, Agatha can feel it, but she doesn’t turn. Beside her, Rio sighs softly again and they turn into town. Agatha wants to go back in time and do it all different, to appreciate what she had more, to fix herself before her habits are too ingrained to stop. Before her walls were too high to climb.

    But she can't bear the thought of being turned away. This is just a pretend situation, Rio doesn't truly care for her, anymore, she's just a good actor. That's all this is and that's all it can remain.

    The Night We Met Chapter Summary Agatha and Rio have always been inevitable.

    Chapter Notes Been feeling sick lately, damn weather change, so sorry that I haven't been replying to y'alls comments.

    I really tossed up making this chapter even longer and adding to their story, but keeping it shorter ended up winning out. This is just a flashback into their past to help us understand who they are now, after all. I honestly felt really unsure about all of this chapter but no matter how many times I read back through it I couldn't figure out what I didn't like, so here it is anyway.

    As always, love and appreciate y'alls support for this fic!

    Disclaimer: I have nothing to do with law (so why the fuck do I keep writing about lawyers??) so there are going to be inconsistencies with Agatha's job that aren't realistic, just bear with me on those. (and if you're a lawyer and want to correct me on something hmu on tumblr I'll fix that shit in a heartbeat)

    With a heavy groan of agitation that she barely suppresses to an appropriate volume, Agatha plants herself in the long line of the grocery store. Her basket hangs heavy at her side, laden with enough wine for a small frat party and barely any food at all. She stares ahead at the slow-moving line, her agitation simmering under the surface.

    The dim yellow lights flicker above her once or twice, and a few people ahead of her look up at them. At this hour it is only college students at the store buying snacks because they're too high to function without them - or drunk. Or it's the odd person who ran out to get last minute items for their dinners. Most baskets hold milk, paper towels, things like that. Agatha's hangs a little heavier at the sight of the light burdens they carry.

    Mustering another groan of agitation as she watches the clerk mis-scan something for the fifth time, she shifts on her feet. She doesn't want to be out here. She'd rather be at home, pretending she's done with all the bullshit she's been dealing with all week. It's late, and her options were either go straight home with nothing waiting for her, or buck up and go to the store. For some damn reason she happened to pick the busiest store at this hour - because its the only one still open.

    Someone clears their throat behind her and Agatha slowly turns around, glaring at the older gentleman as he pointedly looks at her. His disgruntlement with her actions are not her problem, she decides easily.. Agatha scoffs at him and faces ahead. To her relief, someone leaves the store and she is one step closer.

    After a long semester of back-to-back classes and diligence, Agatha wanted nothing more than to curl up in her chair at home and submit herself to a long night of absolutely nothing. As much as she loves the pursuit of knowledge, even she has her limits, and she’s pushing it ruthlessly. Two years left. Four behind her. A break in between. It feels never ending. She'll be in school until the day she dies, she swears it.

    A few people split out of the line and give up, the tired employee scanning as slow as a damn snail. Eleven at night shouldn’t hold this many people in one store.

    It feels as though it takes hours for her to finally be the one at the register, and she spares one glance for the wall of cigarettes behind the cashier before snapping out her demand. He moves sluggishly, grabbing the wrong one and Agatha snaps at him to grab the right one. The cashier couldn’t care any less about her, but he grabs the one she says and plops it down, eyeing her as he rattles off the total.

    Agatha swiftly leaves into the chilly night air, her shoulder already aching from the weight of the bag. She tucks it into her elbow and lights the cigarette as soon as she steps outside, pinching it to the corner of her mouth as she shoves the carton and her lighter into her pocket and starts walking.

    Police lights flicker down the street in the direction she’s heading, and Agatha slows her pace out of curiosity. There is a cop car with a dark red sedan ahead of it, and the officer steps away from the driver’s side and the red car takes off slowly. The officer, a woman, tucks something into her pocket and looks up at her as Agatha meanders by.

    “Those will kill you,” she says, her voice a subdued husk. Agatha frowns at her.

    “So could you, what’s your point?” Agatha shoots back, intending to walk away from the woman.

    “Going to a party or something?” the officer gestures to Agatha bag of wine and she steps up onto the sidewalk close to her.

    “I don’t think that’s any of your concern, is it?” Agatha says harshly.

    The woman smiles at her in amusement and clicks a pen absently at her side. She cocks her head and looks Agatha up and down and a flush tempts at Agatha’s neck. Annoyed or not, she is not immune to the attention of an attractive woman. Instead of letting it show, Agatha maintains her glowering expression with a haughty air of impatience. The woman cocks her hip and rests her forearm on top of her utility belt. She’s in black slacks and button down of the police, a light jacket covering her and a tie around her neck. Black hair is tied neatly behind her head, exposing her sharp jawline.

    “It is if I’m under the suspicion that you’re off to shove liquor down the throats of the underage,” she replies easily. There’s a tilt to her lips, like this is amusing to her for some reason.

    Agatha eyes the nameplate of the officer as she shifts to face her head on. Alright, fine, she'll do this. Her tongue could always use sharpening. Agatha might be the last person to entertain dull conversation, but as she reads Vidal and curls her lip at the woman’s arrogance, she indulges herself.

    “You have no evidence to make that assumption. How many innocents have you prayed on tonight already? A little too bored of sitting behind a desk smelling like a slaughterhouse all day?” Agatha whips out retort after retort, her words just in the right side of dangerous and the officers dark eyes glint with something just as dangerous. The comment that leans just close enough to outright calling her a pig makes the woman breathe out a laugh, making her eyes sparkle in the lights.

    “You’re clearly in college, it’s a safe assumption,” she says casually. The officer scans the streets around them as if Agatha is boring her. The insult makes her seethe, and the amused lilt to the woman's lips is just the cherry on top.

    Agatha has spent years proving herself to people that don't matter. By now she should know how to let comments like these roll off her back and mean nothing, but something about this woman gets under her skin. The thought that she could just walk away doesn't even cross her mind. Agatha, for some reason, feels compelled to stay right where she is and argue.

    “Clearly?” Agatha echoes, trying to keep her cool. “What the hell are you implying?”

    “You look young, that’s all,” she shrugs. Vidal looks her up and down again, tilts her head, and then shrugs once more as she meets her eyes. “And stressed. And you’re smoking, have a bag of wine enough to kill yourself with," she points with a lazy finger at the bag hanging from the crook in Agatha's elbow and then gestures that same hand up her body, "and you’re arrogant. Ergo; college student. Let me guess; you want to be a lawyer?”

    “I don’t want to be , I am ,” Agatha corrects. She’s a paralegal, but this damn cop doesn’t need to know that. This is just another step in the ladder for her. “Don’t bite off more than you can chew, there’s more to me than just lawyer .” She curls her upper lip and as a last parting comment she spitefully shoots back, "I'm an attorney." It's a lie, she's not exactly finished with that type of schooling, yet, but she will be, soon. Besides, she's never going to see this woman again.

    Agatha rolls her eyes and plucks the cigarette from her lips and starts to walk away haughtily. Fucking cops. Her heels click on the pavement with a satisfying finality and she tosses her hair from her eyes as she blows out smoke.

    “I’d like to find out what more there is to you,” the officer says, keeping pace with her in such a casual way that it grates on her nerves. Agatha glances at her in annoyance, looking her up and down. “Dinner tomorrow.

    Seven. I’ll pick you up.”

    “I don’t think so,” Agatha scoffs. She glances at the woman and wants to shove her into the brick wall beside them. “Get lost, officer , or I’ll cite harassment.”

    It’s annoying that Agatha thinks she looks really pretty in the fading red and blue lights behind them. They make her eyes sparkle just right and her smile is . . . it makes Agatha’s mind shut off for a moment is what it is.

    “Okay.” Vidal keeps walking next to her, offering a dazzling smile. “Have a good night, lawyer .”

    Agatha is briefly stupefied by her brilliant grin now that it’s tacked on with her retreat, and merely manages to nod curtly and keep walking, smoking as Vidal walks away, looking her up and down as if they’ll see each other again soon.

    It's an odd interaction, but as Agatha relaxes between semesters and prepares for the next, it’s pushed to the back of her mind. She does, sometimes, think of that arrogant officer with a bright, beautiful smile and a low drawl, but she has bigger fish to fry.

    “Jen, I don’t care how many times you ask, I’m not going to that stupid ball,” Agatha snarls quietly into her phone. She’s gritting her teeth against her sisters silly little ploy to get her back to Salem. It’s easily been a decade since she’s been home – since she’s been kicked out and ostracized – and she doesn’t plan on ending that streak any time soon.

    Those wounds are still too fresh to pick at. Not quite scars but not quite scabs, they still ache and hurt as if they’re brand new and as much as she might be able to hide it, she can’t lie to herself forever. She hadn’t wanted to leave, she wasn’t ready to be out on her own and off in this bright new world that quickly turned dark. Agatha had known that the world wasn’t perfect, but she was quickly forced to realize what exactly that meant when she was faced with the prospect of no money, no job, and no idea where to go next.

    Needless to say, she had a rough couple of years before getting to where she is now, and she’ll claw out her own eyes before letting her traitorous family revel in some of her glory.

    “Don’t you think it’s time to come home?” The question makes Agatha want to punch something, but instead she slides her tongue over her teeth, imagining if they were sharper, tougher what she might be able to cut open with them.

    “I am home,” Agatha says coldly, glancing around at her empty office.

    “Stop wasting your breath, Jen, go back to Mommy .” She hangs up the phone and presses her finger to her temple at the ache residing there.

    Someone knocks on her door and Agatha looks up, blindsided at the casual, sly smile of the woman on the other side of the threshold. She’s familiar in a way that Agatha can’t immediately place, but she stands up anyway, offering her social smile.

    “Hello” the stranger greets, looking her up and down. “I have an appointment with you, but I’m a little early.”

    The woman wears a pair of black slacks, a white, crisp shirt tucked into them. Black hair is loosely done up behind her head in a messy bun. On her belt is a badge clipped tight. This must be a detective. Right. She called a few days ago saying she needed an appointment to discuss some legal matters, without disclosing what those were. Not all that unusual, normally, except now she’s staring at a detective and it’s a little different than some random person calling for help.

    “That's fine, close the door and have a seat,” Agatha tells her. She narrows her eyes, struggling to place the familiarity of her dark hair and soft, depthless eyes.

    The detective closes the door and comes towards her, sitting down across from her. She leans back and crosses one leg, the definition of relaxed. Not many people come into her office and display such casualness. It’s either worry, stress, tears, or some kind of shady look. Sitting down, Agatha picks up a pen idly, gesturing towards her as she tugs a notebook in front of herself.

    “I’m of the impression that the department provides you with legal counsel, why are you here?” Agatha starts.

    “I didn’t come here about my job,” the detective says. She looks. .

    .amused.

    “Then I fail to see the goal of this meeting,” Agatha says flatly. She eyes her again, and the detective smiles pretty at her and click .

    Agatha didn’t recognize her in the light of day streaming in through her windows and without red and blue lights lighting up her expression. In the suit she looks calmly imposing, and Agatha looks at her with disguised interest, seeing the same reflected back at her.

    “Officer ,” she tacks on as an afterthought, and Vidal’s smile widens.

    “I knew you’d remember me,” she says, entirely too pleased with herself. “I came here for a date.”

    “A date?” Agatha echoes, unimpressed. “Which date, detective? There are too many to count in this millennia.”

    Detective Vidal – my, she’s moved up, hasn’t she? How long has it been since that night outside of the grocery store? – sighs and rolls her neck. She shifts slightly, readjusting in her seat before looking at Agatha with a calmer expression, more subdued than before.

    “I’d like to ask you out, lawyer ,” she says. Something about the way she says it makes Agatha tilt her head. “Don’t tell me lawyers don’t date.”

Harder still to look at her and not feel that warming blossom of affection – she won’t let herself think of it as anything else. It’s just. Old wounds. Agatha thinks back on that creeping blush that night in the car and her heart shatters into a million pieces. Rio moves on obliviously, and for an unknown reason, it pisses her off. Here's this woman that she raised a child with, and she's. . . what? Feeling sorry for Agatha? Is that why she kept this from her? If Rio is seeing someone, she should've made it known. Agatha. . .well, she doesn't know what she would have done differently, but this would have been different.

    “Wanda, you’re not going to believe this.”

    “Agatha? What are you doing with – who’s phone is this?” Wanda’s voice rings through, and Agatha hears idle voices in the background. Oh, right. Christmas Eve, her beloved in-laws are still over.

The clerk passes them over and Agatha smiles flatly in thanks as she pays and walks out. Moving in a frenzy, Agatha rips open one of the cartons and glares around her as she pins the phone to her shoulder. She tucks the cartons under her arm and yanks out a cigarette, and every time she tries to light it the wind snuffs it out while she waits for Wanda’s return.

    “What is that sound?” Wanda asks in confusion, after Agatha’s third failed attempt to light it.

    “I’m smoking,” she says, her voice muffled slightly. Successfully lit, she plows onward as if she never stopped. “She’s seeing someone, and after all that we did. We kissed. She came into my shower and yet she’s with someone. I can’t believe it! The fucking traitor.”

    Wanda is being levelheaded.

    “Some fucking loyalty.”

    Agatha is not.

    “Okay, you need to stop smoking and stop spiraling, I think maybe just take a walk.”

    “I can’t, I told her I’d sign her warrant,” Agatha grumbles. She still needs to call the local branch and hope they’re even open, still. It’s easily been ten minutes since she walked away from the alley, longer since she started the call. “I don’t have time for a walk.”

    Looking both ways, Agatha storms across the street smoking as though she's having the last one of her life. Her mind is buzzing and her fingers tingle with the many emotions within her. How dare she, Agatha snarls to herself.

    “Breathing exercises,” Wanda says firmly.

    As she’s walking towards the restaurant, she sees the side door open.

    Agatha shoves the cartons into her pockets and hopes it isn’t too noticeable. She snuffs out the cigarette and steps on it as she quickens her pace.

    “I have to go. Don’t call this number back,” Agatha says quickly. There are sounds of refute on the other line, but she ignores it and ends the call, walking up to the door in time for Rio to spot her.

She’s watched the whole walk through the room up until Rio leads her into a private office and closes the door. The agents have done a decent job at making the back office of a restauraunt serve as their office. The room smells of stale coffee and too many colognes mixing together. Agatha wrinkles her nose in distaste at them, glancing at it all through the window of Rio's office.

    “We need to see if we can even get you approved to do this before we begin, I have the number for the office already.” Rio sits down at the made- up desk and types away on her laptop riddled with countless open documents.

She sits down and is careful to keep distance between the two of them as she crosses one leg over the other and waits for the number to be rattled off to her. Still using Rios phone, Agatha ignores the subsequent texts coming through in favor of focusing entirely too hard on the eight digits read off to her.

It’s a forty-five minute phone call and Agatha wishes she would’ve just gone down to their office, but too many people there know her as the mayors daughter and their suspicions would rise.

    The day is gone in the blink of an eye. Rio rapidly explains their probable cause, pulling up documents and showing Agatha receipts and security footage. Her hands move as she talks and Agatha barely registers anything she’s saying, she’s so enraptured by her.

    “This document here is calling for-” Rio is gesturing to a piece of paper with words emblazoned on the top that Agatha has seen too many times in her own career.

    Raising her eyebrows, the edges of Rio’s lips twitch up and her eyes sparkle. It only infuriates her more.

    “Just checking that you’re paying attention, they’re the reason I’m even here,” Rio murmurs. She’s pointing at a line on the page and keeps eye contact with Agatha as she draws her finger down. “What I’m trying to tell you is the information they found regarding her funds. Your name is knocked off the inheritance from your father, and so is Jen’s, leaving it all to Evanora.”

    That gives her pause.

    “Including Jen, really?” Agatha leans over and skims through the document, murmuring in surprise. Rio pulls that paper aside and sets a new one in front of her quietly and Agatha braces her hands on the desk, leaning away to read it better. “Didn’t bring my glasses,” she murmurs in explanation.

    Rio hums and Agatha keeps reading.

    “Fine, consider me satisfied on that front, what else?” Agatha prompts, straightening up. She lightly tugs her shirt down that had ridden up and looks over at Rio, annoyed at how perfectly put together the woman is right now. “Fucker,” she mumbles, clearing her throat.

    Rio eyes her with glittering interest, but clearly didn’t hear what she said, or they’d be in a battle of wills, without a doubt.

    “The money from the campaigns earlier in the year has been laundered through personal accounts, we’ve been told via some. . .anonymous tips,”

    Rio says with a light shrug. “This warrant is for her bank records. We believe. . . “ Rio goes on to spew information about their probable cause for all of this and Agatha glares at her before looking down at the paperwork Rio is talking her through.

    Agatha tries several times to force herself to focus, her job hinges on things like this and if Rio is wrong – Rio isn’t wrong, Agatha knows it in her gut – then her career gets re-evaluated. It’s important that she listens and digest the information, but she can’t.

Thankfully, not the same bar that Agatha had grabbed Rio and pulled the woman into herself at. Both of them order water to drink without asking, despite the fact that Agatha would rather down several glasses of amber, right now. Rio is an endless wave of energy, and Agatha almost feels like she’s being drained by that. Her own mind is spinning, but not about this case like Rio’s. No, Agatha is constantly thinking of the lo- affection in her chest for the other woman.

Pretending they don’t exist and convincing herself she’s better off without them. . .but this feeling that sinks low in her throat and stays there as she looks at Rio. . . .It keeps reminding her over and over of them. Their little family. Rio with Nicky. Carrying him on his shoulders. Rio sidling up behind Agatha in the kitchen, softly grabbing her hips and pulling her in for a brief, seductive kiss. The constant touches between them.

    It hurts.

    It hurts so much to look across the table at the woman she. . .she cares greatly for and feel that emotion low in her chest and know that it doesn’t belong to her. She doesn’t have that right anymore and Rio is silently eating across from her as if nothing is wrong, typing something into her phone, looking around in thought and then typing again.

    “I’ve been wondering something,” Rio says out of nowhere, pulling her head from her phone. She tilts her head at Agatha, eyes unreadable.

    Wondering is never a good idea. Something always goes wrong when people start wondering. Especially when said person is Rio Vidal no doubt wondering about Agatha.

    “Why did you come up here this year? You haven’t, since . . .” Rio says, trailing off instead of insinuating them.

    Agatha is about to ask how she would know that she hasn’t been here, but several idle, quiet conversations with Jen play in her mind about her absence. She slides her tongue over her teeth and shrugs, crossing her arms.

    “Change of pace,” she says. Agatha looks Rio up and down. “I’m surprised you were able to avoid my mother this whole time. How long have you been here working on this?”

    “A few weeks, by now,” Rio says. She hesitates and then leans her arms on the table between them, searching Agatha’s face. “Are you okay?”

    “What?” Agatha balks with a scoff.

    Is she okay? Not by a mile, but what right does she have to ask that?

    “You’ve been off today, from the rest of the trip.” Her eyes narrow, sharp as ever. “Is something wrong?”

    “Rio, this is not some trip, this is a mutually beneficial transaction,” Agatha reminds her, convincing herself that maintaining an emotional distance is better. Rio has someone waiting for her, after all, she can’t. . . she can’t let herself forget that. “And right now, we are working together. Acting in some part that neither of us know how to play anymore.”

Rio leans back into the booth and lays her arm across the back of it and she’s posed so naturally sexy that Agatha doesn’t want to look away.

When those dark eyes flick to her, Agatha feels something long since dormant lift its head inside of her. “That kiss felt fake, for certain. Not an ounce of -”
