    She’s well aware that Rio is dragging this entire probable cause thing out. This never takes this long. Certainly not a whole day. Whatever she’s plotting, Agatha doesn’t want a single piece of it. At this point she wants to wrap it all up and go home.

    (the thought strikes her, then, that nothing is stopping her from going home, but she promptly pretends she never had that thought in a fatal bid for self- destruction)

    They head back to Venatoni’s and Rio has her hand at the low of Agathas back. It’s a casual embrace, and forced, no doubt, because of this act they’re in and Agatha embraces it, albeit stiffly. The audacity of this woman at her side brings that fury to a roar. Agatha feels it tip its head back and claw and scrape and its a familiar beast to tame and one she hasn’t had to since she sat in a therapists office in Westview and screamed about intimacy to a woman who’d long since stopped giving it.

    They step inside and its a muted chaos within the room. People are flitting between desks, carrying papers and some are sat behind monitors with tired eyes in the shitty lighting of the back office. Agatha separates herself from Rio as they walk down the aisle of made up desks, and the chill of her hand no longer being on her makes her feel so abruptly empty.

    Agatha can’t stand this hot and cold she’s doing to herself. It’s going to drive her insane. It aches, now that she realizes she still loves Rio, only. . .she can’t have her. Agatha normally isn’t above that kind of manipulation it would take to get rid of this mysterious other woman, but she has a job that unfortunately hinges on reputation. So, that goes out the window, and Agatha is left with the crippling realization that it won’t happen. Rio isn’t hers. Hasn't been for a long time but this concrete kind of confirmation makes it real in a way she didn’t expect.

    The fight leaves her all at once, as they walk back into that small office of Rio’s.

    It’s a wonder she remembers how to sign her own name when they get back to Venatoni’s and Rio puts a document before her, and when it’s done Rio barely says a word, she bolts upright and slings the door to her temporary office open. The room beyond surges with energy and people are bustling this way and that and things are happening and Agatha. . .

    Agatha is still sitting in that stupid stiff chair in a temporary office, staring at the temporary desk, thinking about what they could have been. What she could have done differently. Feeling sad for herself for no other reason than she’s tired of feeling angry.

    When has that anger ever served her properly?

    “We’re going to the ball as normal,” Rio announces, returning from talking to her team. Agatha blinks a few times and looks up as she walks over to her desk and scuffles through it for a moment. “Will you be fine with that?”

    “Yeah, yeah, that’s fine,” Agatha manages to reply.

    God. Now she’s thinking of Rio in a dress. Dancing with Rio. Not being able to kiss her.

    “Great, I need to go, we’re doing a briefing and then we’re splitting up.” Rio must’ve found whatever she was looking for because she stands up straight and looks at her. There’s a gentle smile on her lips and a distracted haze over her eyes. “Alright?”

    “Perfect,” Agatha agrees.

    Rio nods and leaves Agatha to go to whatever briefing they’re giving. She glances out the window after a while of sitting in silence and in the other room she sees Rio standing in front of a projector screen, she’s the one giving the briefing.

    Pride swells in Agatha’s chest, thinking back on their early conversations about where life might take them. Where they might want to be someday.

    She’s done it, she realizes. Rio has all she wanted now. The job she thought she’d never get. A home. Girlfriend.

    It crushes her to realize she’s not apart of that, that she wasn’t there for that journey to support and love her through it. That ache doesn't fade as easily as the loneliness had, it settles deep into her bones in the empty, cavernous space that was once occupied by something good.

    Watching her in her element briefly garners a spike of envy, but it’s quickly quieted by that blossoming bud of affection that’s too big and too important to name. She looks beautiful. Strong. Powerful up there.

    The briefing ends and the group disperses quickly. Rio grabs something from the projector and then returns to the office and Agatha doesn’t even bother to try and not look as though she was just watching her. How could she not? Rio is entrancing.

    “Agatha? Are you ready to go get changed?” Rio asks, hovering in the doorway.

    Rio’s mind is moving a thousand miles a minute, Agatha knows, so she simply nods and stands up. The drive back to the mayoral mansion is filled with the gentle drumming of Rio’s fingers on the steering wheel. Agatha stares out the window and wonders just how the hell she’s going to get through tonight.

    To Fall, To Fly Chapter Summary Agatha and Rio attend the Yuletide ball.

    Chapter Notes Yeah, uhhhhh im realizing i didnt do any research into when Yule actually is and if it lines up with christmas or not. Sorry bout that, but uhhh this is whats happening in the fic, now. Also I have never been to Salem, this is made up fanfiction Salem land, so we're sticking with that.

    Again, I'm editing this while super duper sick so like, if some things in here make no sense yall gotta tell me so i can fix it hopefully next week when I am better.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Getting ready is a little awkward. They’ve been naked in front of each other a surprising amount, recently, but getting dressed intentionally in front of each other feels too intimate. Agatha is standing in front of her bag only for a few seconds, as she realizes this, before Rio takes the bathroom, preventing her from having to decide what to do.

    As much as Agatha would’ve enjoyed seeing Rio naked once again – and in her wildest dreams, maybe skip the ball altogether – she knows this is for the best. She has someone, Agatha reminds herself, and Agatha isn’t that someone.

    To her relief, Agatha is ready before Rio, and with the house empty she leaves her with the bathroom door shut and wanders down to wait in the foyer. She’s standing in front of the lit-up Christmas tree, remembering what it had been like to live here, once upon a time, and feel loved.

    It has been a long, long time since she’s felt anything akin to love in this house, but it did exist once upon a time.

    The last Christmas, before Dad’s hunting accident, Jen and Agatha had stayed up late. Snuck around their parents who were talking in the den, and went outside to the greenhouse. They were seventeen and the wonder of Christmas and Santa had worn off a long time ago. Agatha had coerced an older kid in her class to buy her some things, and so there she and Jen ended up; tipsy in the greenhouse on Christmas Eve.

    Agatha had confessed, late in the night, that she felt more attraction to the girls in her class than the boys. Had said something like “id be a lot happier if I could just kiss girls for the rest of my life, they’re better at it, anyway” and Jen had given her this flat look and asked “why can’t you?”.

    It felt easy. It was such a simple answer. Why couldn’t she?

    Agatha is certain Jen doesn’t remember that night.

    They’d snucj back into the house before dawn, had a few, short hours of sleep, and then the whole family was dragged outside to watch the sunrise. Then they were dragged to church later that day, of course, but that last sunrise. . .

    Agatha held so much hope and love for her future, and her life, then. This question that had hounded her for most of her youth, countered by one simple question. Why can’t she? Jen had made it all so simple and it relieved Agatha of a burden she hadn’t even realized she was carrying.

    Before that tipsy confession, she hadn’t put any thought into what she felt. Had kept it pushed to the back of her mind, hidden and forgotten, and had surrendered to the idea of never being able to have that dream and that it would just stay as such forever.

    That morning watching the sunrise, Agatha had seen her whole life ahead of her. Had looked at Jen and sworn they’d always be there for each other. Had felt the love of both father and mother and known she was loved. It was brief, but for awhile, she had it, and Agatha will always secretly cherish that moment in time.

    It all came crashing down a few months later. After dad’s death, Evanora became cruel and cold. Agatha had been caught with a girl in the house, kissing her without any fears or worries. Agatha had learned what her mothers wrath felt like that day, and it burned on her cheek for years to come. The day she turned eighteen she was chased away. Forced from the only home she’d ever known and Jen was forced to witness it all.

    Scared into silence, Jen watched it all and learned that day to never show her true self to Evanora Harkness, a lesson Agatha learned the hard way for the both of them.

    At least she bore the weight of that burden, and not Jen. Agatha would hate to see Jen feel that pain.

    The horrible memories outweigh the good, but now as she stands before the mantle, looking at photographs she’s no longer in – hasn’t been on this mantle in years - she yearns for a life that might’ve been better. One where she could’ve shown Nicky the beautiful sunrise on the dock out back.

    Surrounded him with Rio, engulfed him in their love.

    At first, her expression is bright and earnest, but the more Agatha talks, Rio’s face falls. The arm around her slackens but doesn’t fall away entirely. Agatha swallows thickly, pausing to let Rio step away slightly and then continues.

    “It is important to remember our roots, and where we all came from, we would not be where we are today without those who built the foundation, we all stand on.” There’s an idle clapping around as Evanora looks out at the crowd with that stupid picture-perfect politician's smile.

    “I am grateful that my own daughters understand such a thing,” Evanora says, and Agatha wants to spew out hateful words from the crowd, instead she stays by Rio, talking quietly.

    “Without them and their support and understanding for what I do, I could not stand before you now, certain that I am doing what is right.”

    Agatha clenches her hand into a fist.

    “Let us welcome the next year with grace and warmth, and- “Agatha tunes out the rest of Evanora’s speech as she pulls away from Rio at last.

    Rio is staring straight ahead, her eyes glistening. She looks away from Agatha and then turns away from her entirely. Agatha doesn’t watch her leave; she turns and walks towards the entrance. She struggles to not cry, to not just sprint away as she reaches the edge of the ballroom floor.

    “Agatha, are you okay?”

    She looks up, surprised to see Wanda standing in front of her. Sans her coat, Wanda is holding a champagne glass in one hand, inching closer to her.

    “Thank you for coming up here to check on me,” Agatha says sincerely.

    Wanda places her hand on Agatha’s arm and Agatha twists back to see Evanora watching her. She faces Wanda again and the woman is looking behind her. "I need to go."

    "I'll drive," Wanda insists. She moves, setting her glass on a nearby table.

    “No, don’t, I need. . .I need to be alone.” Agatha strides away without waiting for her response, quickly leaving the building entirely.

    The valet recognizes her, thankfully, and her car is brought around quickly. Agatha hopes Rio does actually have a car, here, because she climbs into her own and drives away.

    The town is empty, seemingly abandoned as she rolls through the streets.

    Agatha doesn’t know where she’s driving, but it’s aimless and she can’t think straight. Her mind is reeling with the last hour of events, and she turns a corner sharply.

    Agatha wipes at her eyes in frustration, the conversation playing over in her mind, overlapping and tearing her up from the inside out.

    “What’s this about?” Agatha asked sharply, carefully stepping into the room.

    Evanora shuts the door behind her, and Agatha narrows her eyes on the man at the desk. He’s the man who hosted the party they attended not even two nights ago, and he looks at Agatha with such condescension that Agatha wants to hurl something at him.

    “You’re wading in waters too deep for your purview,” he says. “Working with the FBI isn’t something I anticipated from you.”

    “I didn’t break any laws, as I’m sure you’re well aware,” Agatha snaps. She crosses her arms and Evanora circles around from behind her. Agatha abruptly understands what a bug with it’s wings caught in the hands of an unsuspecting child feels like.

    “Are you sure?” He asks, and he stares at Agatha for several beats, likely trying to give her a chance to say something confessional. When she simply stares back, he twists behind himself and picks up a piece of paper. “You and your wife have been busy, haven’t you?”

    That makes her freeze.

    “I believe part of your oath is to ‘ To faithfully and impartially carry out the duties of the office’ , is that right?” He asks, as if he doesn’t already know the answer. Agatha flicks her tongue over her teeth. “Regardless of rich or poor?”

    “I know the oath, you don’t need to recite it to me,” she snaps. “Is this a review of my duties or does this conversation have a point?”

    “That is the point,” Evanora says. “You unlawfully worked directly with the FBI on a case, knowingly and willfully, and all to do what? To earn your place between a woman’s legs, what else-“

    “What case would that be, mom?” Agatha interrupts, knowing how much Evanora hates that. She’d rather not touch on the other subject.

    The woman narrows her eyes in fury but merely breathes in sharply and keeps going.

    “A false case with no grounds nor evidence,” Evanora says. “You would throw your career away over this?”

    “Justice, mom, is why I’m doing this,” Agatha snaps. “Not even you can hide from the laws that contain us.”

    “Is that what you think?” Evanora asks, and she moves over to the desk.

    Something drops in Agatha’s stomach. “Illegally obtained evidence.

    Unlawfully acquired statements. There is more, need I go on?” Evanora glances at her.

    “What do you want?” Agatha doesn’t let herself fidget, or shift on her feet, she forces herself to hold Evanora’s gaze without flinching.

    “Leave. Leave and take all that you know of this case with you,” she tells her. Agatha swallows. She opens her mouth to talk but Evanora isn’t done. “Separate from that leech attached to you. She will do you no good.”

    “That’s what all of this is about?” Agatha scoffs out a laugh and tips her head back. “You want me to break up with Rio?”

    “You are an abomination!” Evanora snaps and Agatha shakes her head. “I will not have my family drag my name through the mud. You are a Harkness, and if you wish to keep that name on your career, you will leave her.”

    Agatha finds it curious that Evanora seems to have done research on her yet, doesn’t know of their separation. Despite knowing that they already are separated, Agatha won’t give an inch to Evanora.

    “Rot in hell. If you want to sue me then send the paperwork to my office, if you want to try and take me down for defamation, then go ahead and try, you will not find a thing on me that can fuel that case, and I will take you down while you try to erase me,” Agatha says firmly. She scoffs and looks between them. “I hope you have a good lawyer.”

    Satisfied with herself and set to seek out Rio and tell her of all that’s happened, Agatha turns to leave. This will surely only help Rio’s case, and Agatha would love to see Evanora rot in a cell somewhere. It’s her word against a Mayor’s, but she has good sway, she hasn’t spent her whole life on this career for nothing.

    “Before you leave, there’s one more thing I think you’ll want to hear,”

    Evanora calls behind her.

    Agatha scoffs, her hand on the doorknob, but she looks back.

    “Oh? Want to cite a bible verse- “

    “It’s about Jen.”

    Agatha wipes at her eyes as she tears down the road. Props where they’re due; Evanora always knows how to cut her deep and make it burn. She can’t believe her mother would stoop so low. She can, but it hurts so badly that she doesn’t want to.

    After so many years, she didn’t think she could keep hurting like this. There has to be an end to it all, but she must have pissed off the fates a long time ago because they keep yanking the ground from beneath her feet and –

    Ground that’s slick with ice she doesn’t see in time and Agatha lets off the gas and turns into the spin of the car. She’s got one hand on the wheel, viciously spinning it into the turns and then countering them and lightly pressing the brakes but the whole road is now ice and she turns, catches a patch of dry ground and the whole car jerks and shudders and –

    Something beeps, and the cadence follows her into sleep.

    Distorted voices echo in her ears. Agatha hears that beeping again and something jostles her. Blinking deliriously, Agatha struggles to clear her vision and look around.

    White. Red. Black.

    The colors bleed and smudge and she blinks again and there’s a pale face hovering over her as someone pushes at her chest. She feels the touch and looks down, something glints and there’s - is that glass?

    It’s hard to breathe. There’s a pressure on her chest that makes her wheeze and her vision blurry. Agatha’s body doesn’t feel like her own. Something – too many things – hurt but she can’t pin down where and what and it’s dizzying to try and figure out.

    The edges of her vision darken and Agatha’s feels like she’s spinning.

    “Agatha!” The name snaps her attention to her left and Agatha balks as Rio’s face comes into perfect focus. She says something else, but Agatha’s eyes begin to roll back, and she exhales roughly.

    Her breathe sounds wet.

    Agatha thinks she wakes up two more times.

    Once, she’s staring up at white walls passing her by rapidly. There’s something on her face and it wheezes and gasps – or maybe she’s making that sound – and there’s a figure keeping pace with her. Turning her head, Agatha doesn’t think twice about what she sees, it fits. It works.

    Rio is running alongside her – she’s on a gurney, in a hospital, is she going into surgery? – and the womans beautiful dress sways with each careful step. Something warms Agatha’s heart at that, and she finds herself smiling as her eyes close again.

    The second time, she’s still delirious. Now she’s in a room with blinds drawn shut and that steady beeping drones on. Agatha hates that sound. Has heard it too much in her short life and if she never hears it again, she’ll be happy.

    Beside her, with the world's most uncomfortable looking chair, is Rio. She’s curled up sideways in the chair, brought right up next to the hospital bed. Rio’s hand is loosely holding Agatha’s, and she thinks she sees tear tracks on her cheeks.

    Agatha relaxes with a feeling of safety and warmth and falls back into sleep without any more thought.

    Waking up completely lucid at last, Agatha winces at the dryness in her mouth and the fuzziness that comes without brushing her teeth. She rubs her tongue against the back of her teeth and opens her eyes, blinking several times to dispel the haze.

    Rio is curled into the chair beside her, her arm tucked under her jaw and her mouth hanging open slightly, soft snores drifting out of her mouth. Their hands are no longer clasped, Rio’s lays limply on the bed and Agatha carefully presses her hand to the bed and sits up.

    She winces at a pain in her chest and looks down to find gauze wrapped tightly around her torso. There’s a big square patch of gauze secured to her, high on the left side of her chest, just inside her shoulder, and Agatha touches it tenderly. It stings and she takes a moment to catalogue herself, feeling what hurts and what doesn’t.

    There’s a tender spot on her left brow, her jaw aches oddly, and her shoulder twinges when she pushes herself more upright again. Overall, she’s not doing terribly, but the fact that she’s gone into surgery doesn’t bode all that well.

    Glancing at Rio again, she sees her curled up in her coat, the dress beneath, and everything rushes back to her. Evanora’s threats and her talking to Rio during the woman’s speech. She shouldn’t be here. She can’t. It’s all going to end if she doesn’t get out of this quickly. Agatha needs to clean this up before anything happens. Before she says something she shouldn’t.

    Reeling, Agatha looks at Rio’s face and startles as she sees brown eyes watching her already.

    “How do you feel?” Rio asks, her voice hoarse. She carefully unfurls herself, wincing softly as she moves to sit properly in the chair.

    “What. . .what time is it?” Agatha asks instead.

    She feels like shit, but that should be obvious, she’s in a hospital.

    Rio looks at her watch and then yawns big.

    “Nearly three.”

    She stands up and stretches entirely, taking her time popping out the kinks in her neck and back, and then she settles back down into her chair and the previously sleepy visage of Rio is replaced with the fully awake and very much focused Special Agent Vidal as Rio spreads her knees and braces her elbows on them, pinning Agatha in place with her eyes.

    “When you are awake enough for it, we need to talk,” Rio says seriously.

    Agatha swallows and glances past her out the window at a doctor passing by.

    “How are you here? Don’t they have visiting –“

    “I don’t always follow the rules, I used some excuse about special protection for a high-profile case, they didn’t argue when I also told them I’m your wife,” Rio says. A mischievous plot glints in her eyes, one that Agatha can’t decipher.

    “That’s a blatant breach of code and a wild exploitation of duty, not to mention a flagrant conflict of interest,” Agatha counters easily and without thought. She needs to get Rio out of here and then quickly herself. She can’t stay here.

    Rio smirks.

    “Great. You seem awake enough, let’s have that talk now, then.”

    She’d stepped right into Rio’s little ploy, and Agatha wants to applaud her for its simplicity.

    “I don’t think there’s anything else to say,” Agatha says flippantly. Shit. Shit.

    “Oh, I have plenty to say, Harkness.” The steady but deep cadence of her voice makes Agatha look over at her defensively and Rio is still looking at her with that pinning gaze.

    “What-?”

    “That kind of shit you just tried to pull back there? Is not going to fly with me,” Rio continues. Agatha opens and closes her mouth in shock. “I see you, Agatha, and I don’t buy it. Not for a second.”

    “Excuse me?” Agatha shifts to face her slightly better, ignoring the twinge in her back. “What high horse did you ride in on because-“

    “Save it for someone less intelligent,” Rio cuts her off.

    Appalled, Agatha can only stare.

    “What did she tell you?” Rio asks calmly. Agatha shakes her head, struggles to come up with something to say to this that will get her out of this conversation as fast as possible. “What did she threaten you with?”

    “I-she didn’t do anything,” Agatha finally says. She swallows, meeting Rio's eyes without hesitance. “I can’t do this, Rio, that’s what that was about. I can’t pretend anymore, it hurts to be around you and I don’t want to pretend it doesn’t anymore. That’s what that was.”

    Rio’s eyes narrow briefly and then she raises her chin and sits up. She crosses one leg over the other and leans back into her seat. Dark eyes look her over, soften slightly, and then Rio sighs and looks away, tonguing at her cheek and perching her chin on the back of her knuckles. Her foot bounces.

    “You don’t trust me, is that why?” Rio asks quietly. Her eyes flick to Agatha and hesitate before looking away again, searching the room.

    “I don’t want to hurt anymore,” Agatha says softly. Rio frowns lightly and shifts in her seat. She sighs and looks away entirely. “That, I think, puts us on the same page for once, doesn’t it?”

    It’s the right words, but Agatha wishes they weren’t.

    Rio visibly slackens. Her shoulders relax and she turns her head away and down, her foot stops bouncing. Rio swallows thickly and then she stands up. Fixes her coat. Rio looks her over with an unreadable expression. When she meets her eyes, Agatha can’t read her, she’s completely closed herself off and then she gets it.

    ‘I always thought I was immune’ and Agatha understands what she means.

    What Rio felt when Agatha pushed her out of her house.

    The only consolation is that this will all be temporary.

    “If she threatened you in some way, Agatha, you should tell me,” Rio says carefully. “Add it to the list of her crimes, and we can make sure she pays for it. If you think you’re in trouble, let me help you. I have an entire team at my disposal, Agatha. I can protect you.”

    It’s not about protection, but Agatha can’t tell her that. She needs to play this properly, to make it look good. Real.

    For a second, Agatha considers throwing caution to the wind and telling Rio everything. This is most certainly one of the few places Agatha would be able to. Evanora hasn’t managed to dig her claws into every inch of this city and her life, this is a corner protected from it all.

    She almost does when she sees the sincerity in Rio’s eyes. The woman would do everything she could, would push aside this case for her if that-

    “Why are you here?” Agatha blurts. Rio frowns and shifts. “I mean, I thought the plan was to confront her tonight. Shouldn’t you be there?”

    At that Rio looks away and then back at her, her expression guarded once more. Briefly, Rio looks at her stupefied, as if the answer is obvious, but she quickly covers it.

    “I got a call,” she says quietly. Rio glances around the room. “That you were in a wreck. So I came.”

    As simple as that.

    Agatha remembers getting a call just like the one Rio had received, and she aches from the depth of it all. One call, and the both of them came running. Years apart, but they’re still the same.

    “You should get back to your agents, I’m sure they need you,” Agatha says quietly.

    Layered in her words are those she doesn’t have the strength to say aloud but means. ‘ I don’t need you ’ echoes in her head unspoken, and the guilt threatens to consume her as she sees part of Rio shatter.

    “Your friend is in the waiting room, I’ll let her know that she should take you home,” Rio says. Rio takes a step away and hesitates, fixing the lay of her coat before looking slightly over her shoulder, baring her profile to Agatha without looking at her. “Merry Christmas, Agatha.”

    Completely composed, Rio leaves without looking back. When Agatha is certain she can’t be seen anymore she collapses back into the hospital bed with a heavy sigh. She would rather be calling Rio back here and welcoming her in an embrace of some kind, but Agatha’s hands are tied.

    She’ll fix this, soon, just not right now. Now she lays back in the bed and lets her eyes drift shut, exhaustion dragging her down. Tomorrow, maybe, she can fix this.

    Fresh Wounds, New Scars Chapter Summary Rio Vidal will do everything she can for her family.

    Chapter Notes Y'all: Omg we really love this it's so good give us more Me: You promise? (sad eyes) Great, here's more pain.

    SORRY but this had to be done. You'll see.

    Here it is at long last. this is the chapter I had to stop writing and step away from because it made me so sad. not sure if y'all will think the same thing or not, but we'll see. there are happy moments in this chapter if you squint.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Taking the stairs at a quick jog, Rio holds the files tightly in her hand. If she can get these processed before the clerk leaves for the day, she can leave right away. Otherwise she’s of no doubt that she’ll be tasked with something else and her attention has been taken by more important things, lately.

    Unease has settled on her shoulders like an old, familiar blanket. It traipses with her every step she takes throughout the day like a shadow she can’t chase away. Nicky in the hospital bed flashes behind her eyes with every other blink, chased away only by the image of Agatha, her eyes empty as she sits beside him.

    The weight of what she’s doing for her family settles on top of the unease. All of these classes, these countless hours, all of this time spent away will be worth it. It will make things easier for them, and hopefully promise better avenues for Nicky. There is talk of a new process to handle the illness, and if she can get this job. . .

    Hope brightens the path ahead, and Rio strides down the halls, brightening further still as she sees the clerk still at their desk. Rio makes quick work of dropping the files off and talking them over, and in no time she is out of the precinct and heading home.

    The house is dark and quiet when she walks in, but it always is. Rio remembers the days fondly when she would come home at a reasonable hour, be greeted with laughter and playfulness. She holds minor resentment for herself, for isolating herself from her family, but her only consolation is that it will all be worth it.

    It has to be, she’ll do what it takes to make it up to them. Both of them.

    Rio sheds her coat and suit jacket as she locks the door, moving silently through the house. She stops to pet Senor Scratchy on the couch before she goes upstairs, smiling fondly at the small rabbit. Rio shucks her shoes as she pets him, and moves quickly upstairs and pushes silently into their bedroom.

    Moonlight casts in through the open window, and Rio stills in the doorway. The bed is empty, still made from when she left it early this morning, more than likely.

    Moving back through her motions, Rio leaves the house and drives to the hospital, moving bleary eyed through the halls without being questioned until she stops at a dark room. Through the glass she can see Agatha, sat on a chair next to the hospital bed, staring at Nicky as he sleeps.

    Sneaking in isn’t an option, not with how watchful her wife is, so Rio merely enters as quietly as she can to not disturb the silence within. Agatha doesn’t move as she approaches, but she does flinch when Rio touches her shoulder.

    Ignoring the action, Rio squats down beside her, keeping her tough light as she rests her hand on Agatha’s shoulder and looks at her.

    “Sweetheart, you should get some sleep,” Rio says softly. Agatha shifts, but Rio knows it’s only so she can get more comfortable. “This isn’t helping him.”

    “And how would you know?” Agatha snaps softly.

    Rio swallows thickly and looks away. Yeah, how would she know? She’s never here, anymore. It pains her greatly to acknowledge that. Soon. She’ll make it better soon.

    “Because I see him, and I see you,” Rio says. She pauses before continuing, studying Agatha’s focused, frustrated features. “I don’t want to lose you both, Agatha,” she whispers. “Please, just go sleep in a bed.”

    “You’ve already lost us, Rio,” Agatha whispers. Tears are in her eyes.

    “When you decided you didn’t need to be around anymore.”

    “Baby, it isn’t like that, you know it isn’t,” Rio pleads. Moving to kneel and subsequently be closer to her, Rio’s hand sinks down to her lower back.

    “Do I? You’re never home to talk about it, Rio,” Agatha says, her voice low and dark from the effort to not cry. Hearing her speak like that plucks at Rio’s heartstring and makes her own eyes burn.

    “Agatha, neither of us make enough to pay for this forever, I have to do something,” Rio says gently. She rubs her hand up and down Agatha’s back, and is spared a brief glance before Agatha shifts away from her touch.

    It feels like a gunshot to the chest, and Rio pulls her hand away and looks down.

    “Come home, baby, you shouldn’t sleep here, again,” Rio insists.

    Agatha doesn’t look like she should be driving, either. Her eyes are tired and dark, and she looks two seconds away from falling asleep if it weren’t for her own determination to stare at him as if each second will be his last.

    For all they know, it could be, so Rio doesn’t voice any of that.

    “It won’t do him any good to-“

    “Go home, Rio,” Agatha interrupts sharply. Rio doesn’t want to look up at her, but she does anyway, and the look in her eyes?

    If Agatha moving away from her touch felt like a gunshot, then the look in her eyes feel like wide awake open heart surgery. Rio almost staggers back from the weight of that look, but she merely stands up and nods, adjusting her coat tighter around herself.

    “Okay.” She nods and walks away, but only to the nearest waiting room.

    Rio finds a seat out of view, but that gives her a view of the hallway, and she waits. Maybe an hour goes until Rio sees Agatha leave, and Rio stands and walks back to his room, taking her place at his side. The chair is still warm as Rio takes it, and she grabs tightly onto Nicky’s hand and sits at his side, keeping watch over their sweet boy.

    He wakes up fitfully once, his face contorting in anguish and he grabs noncommittally at the wires attached to his chest. Rio gently stops his hands and he opens his eyes and looks at her, rubbing his knuckles into his eyes.

    “Mama?” He croaks.

    “She’ll be right back, baby,” Rio assures him. She stands up to press a kiss to his forehead, smoothing his hair back. “Go back to sleep, it’s alright.”

    Nicky nestles into the blankets and does as asked, immediately falling back to sleep. Rio smiles tiredly at him and sits back down, not far behind him as she watches the hospital monitor peak and dip with his heartbeats. The steady drone of the monitor is easily luring her to sleep, and Rio scoots her chair so its perpendicular with the bed. She reaches through the plastic bar, angles her arm so it will stay put even if it slackens with sleep. Rio leans back, crosses her ankles, and looks over at Nicky, letting him be the last thing she sees before she closes her eyes.

    It’s for him. Agatha. Herself. Her little family. She’ll keep them afloat however she needs to. She can weather this storm; she’s survived worse than Agatha Harkness anger.

    The tests are grueling, and when Rio isn’t studying, she’s running, she’s pushing her body to its limits to ensure she can pass every test they throw at her. With every step in daylight out of her work clothes that aren’t spent with her family, Rio is thinking of how this will help them.

    It will take the weight off their backs and give them a better future.

    Rio stops trying to convince Agatha to sleep in a bed after several months of being told to go home alone, and she forms the routine of sitting in the waiting room until Agatha leaves, but she doesn’t stop to see her first, anymore. Sleep doesn’t come easy for any of them after a year, and on a Thursday well after sunset in winter, Rio comes home to Agatha’s car in the driveway.

    She had stopped to change before going to the hospital, and for a moment she sits in the driveway stunned.

    The pause she takes is brief, and Rio heads inside, carefully listening to find out where Agatha is. The house is quiet, as always. She shuts the door and moves throughout the house, searching for her, and when she rounds the living room from the other hallway, she sees her.

    Agatha is sprawled out on the couch, still in her work clothes, and lightly snoring. There’s a laptop open on the coffee table in front of her, long since fallen asleep just like its owner. Rio smiles at that and hangs her coat up at last.

    She’s careful not to wake her up as she gathers Agatha in her arms and takes her upstairs. It’s frightening to her how light she is now. Rio can’t watch her every hour of the day, but she wishes she could at least see her for lunch, convince her to take care of herself.

    As though she’s holding someone made of glass, Rio sets Agatha on the peeled back covers of the bed they’ve not occupied in months. She takes Agatha’s shoes off and then her own, and she slides in behind her, carefully to not wake her. This might be the only good sleep she’s gotten in . . . well, who knows how long, now?

    It feels good to hold her. Rio feels a little more whole. Being away from her feels like there is a gap inside of her. As though Agatha carved out a space for herself in Rios body, and without her filling it. . . Rio is only half. Half of what she wishes she was.

    Rio squeezes her tighter and presses in as close as she can get, as if she can meld Agatha back into that space inside of herself. Press fit themselves together until everything is okay again.

    In her sleep, Agatha let’s out a bone heavy sigh and she turns in Rios arms to press their fronts together. Agatha’s arms are crossed over her chest and she nestles her head underneath Rios jaw. Tucking into her, as if she can hear Rios thoughts and is doing her best to abide.

    The hope that this is all temporary makes Rio relax into the bed, their legs tangling together. This will be over. Nicky will get better; Agatha will come back to her. She has to. Rio can help, and will help the very second she’s asked.

    Until then she’ll do what Agatha says, what she needs. She listens and obeys. If she needs space, Rio will give it even if it kills her, but she hopes she doesn’t.

    Given time, maybe Agatha will let her in, again.

    The phone ringing wakes the both of them up, and they scatter in a mad dash to answer it. Untangling from each other is a haphazard task of limbs and clothes and Rio, being underneath her, doesn’t get up as fast as she does. Agatha gets to the phone first, her voice rough and wheezy.

    “Hello?” She answers, hair askew and clothes wrinkled. There are lines in her face from the imprint of her pillow.

    Rio sits down on the edge of the bed and tries to rub sleep from her eyes as the call goes on. Agatha looks at her, her expression for once unguarded and not filled with something akin to hate. Rio looks back at her as the person on the other end of the line talks, and Agatha swallows thickly and nods.

    “Okay, I’ll be right there.” She hangs up and sighs, tossing the cordless phone to the bed. “It was the hospital, Nicky woke up, so they wanted to make sure that. . .” Agatha trails off and rubs at her eyes.

    “Alright,” Rio nods. She stands up to get changed, halfway through taking her shirt off – and the thought rises wicked and unbidden that she doesn’t know the last time either of them have kissed nonetheless seen each other naked – when Agatha speaks up.

    “I’m going, you stay,” she says.

    Rio swallows thickly before turning around. Agatha has that wild, heated stare pinned on her and it’s hard to breathe deep and take that vitriol and let it roll off her.

    “Sweetheart, he’s our son, why don’t you want me there?” She asks tiredly. It’s an old conversation, but she can’t stop, won’t stop pushing to break past Agatha’s barriers, to understand her and why this hate is now built between them.

    “You’re never here, anyway, what does it matter to you?” Agatha snaps.

    She huffs and then starts quickly unbuttoning her shirt, yanking fresh clothes out of the closet with the front of it hanging open.

    “Would you sit down so we can actually talk about this, then?” Rio asks, staying still as Agatha walks off into the bathroom. She knows Agatha doesn’t like to be crowded so she keeps her distance.

    “What’s there to talk about, Rio?”

    Rio. Not Detective. Not Officer. No endearments, just her name, and it feels like a curse.

    “What I’m trying to do, Agatha, please, just talk to me,” Rio begs. “Why are you shutting me out? We’re supposed to be here for each other, but I can’t if you won’t let me in.”

    “Don’t try to take it back, now, it’s too late,” Agatha snarls, stepping out of the bathroom in fresh clothes.

    The fact that she left the room to change says everything, and Rio takes half a step back.

    “You abandoned us when it got tough, don’t try to make it into something it isn’t,” she says.

    Agatha leaves, and Rio is staggered by the weight of her words. Abandoned them? No. No, her heart cries and shatters and breaks.

    No, she thinks; I’m doing this for you.

    Rio walks after her in a daze, her broken, wailing heart clouding her vision. Agatha is already out the door and in the car by the time Rio makes it to the first level of the house, and she wants to collapse to her knees and cry her grief to whatever heavens might be watching.

    Instead, she gets in her car and drives to the hospital. Agatha is already in the room, gently soothing Nicky. Rio walks in behind her and Agatha sighs.

    “Rio-“

    “Mother!” Nicky grins, his arms shooting up into the air.

    Rio doesn’t have to force the wide smile on her face, and she takes a step forward. Agatha shifts away from her as Rio stands next to the bed.

    “Hey, sweetie,” she greets. Nicky sits up eagerly, watching her.

    “Did you see the show about the dinosaurs! It’s new!” Nicky beams.

    “I did,” Rio lies. “What was your favorite part?”

    Nicky bounces as he talks about his favorite dinosaurs. He beams and babbles and talks endlessly. Even Agatha, as much as she hates Rio, smiles and laughs with him and even leans into Rios side.

    That something whole promises to fill itself in, then.

    Late one night as she stays at the precinct to study, Rio sighs, bone weary. The room she’s in is empty except for her, outside there are a few people working the graveyard shift that are keeping busy.

    Rio wants to move on autopilot, wants to give everything up and go to that hospital room that holds her entire world. To forget this endless race to get better opportunities, because what’s the point if she doesn’t even get to be with the family she’s trying to help?

    She almost does, one night at the station as she’s staying late to pour over test material. Grabbing her keys, she has her hand on her coat to walk out now and stop it all, but her eyes land on a picture frame on her desk.

    The three of them, smiling into the sunset. Nicky is on Rio’s shoulders, beaming bright and beautiful, sunglasses on Rio’s nose about to slide off because of how Nicky holds her head to stay upright. Agatha is wrapped around Rio, mid laugh and breathtaking even through a 2D image.

    They had talked about dinosaurs and Rio had looked at Agatha and everything slotted into place. All her lonely years meant nothing in the face of this beautiful family of hers. One that she can save by doing this.

    With a heavy sigh, Rio plops back into her seat and keeps studying. They’ll be happy like that again if she keeps this up. They’ll have it again, she knows it.

    The time finally came. Two weeks of grueling tests that she’s been training for well over a year, and then an interview scheduled. She’s passed all the tests so far, but the interview is what everything hinges on. The rest of her career, and her family’s livelihood, depends on how well she can do this.

    Months, nearly years of this is finally reaching a breaking point. She left as soon as she had a time slot, and Rio plans to be back before too long.

    Agatha won’t miss her anyway – the thought makes her ache and hurt, but she pushes it aside, for now. She left a note on the counter, hoping it won’t be buried by the mail that litters the rest of the space – they should really clean, it’s been a while.

    If she gets this, they won’t have to worry about the hospital bills piling up on that counter.

    When she walks into the building, Rio puts her best foot forward. Puts on the perfect visage of exactly who they want to see. Pretends she’s already who they want. She thinks of her family, of what she’s going to do for them as she sits in that stuffy office and convinces them she’d be good for the job, and they agree.

    Driving in to the interview she held all the fear and hopes of the rest of her life, and leaving, she feels lighter.

    She did it.

    Impossibly, somehow, she did it. In a month she’ll be off to the initial courses and then to her first duty. It’ll get better. She knows it.

    This is where it all changes.

    Rio doesn’t bother going home. Her home is all in that hospital room, anyway. The house is empty so there’s no point unlocking the door.

    There’s a purpose to her step as she strides down the familiar Halls and steps into the room. Agatha looks at her and then stands up, whirling towards her. Nicky is asleep, Rio notes.

    “You can’t just show up out of nowhere. I haven’t heard from you in days and now what?” Agatha is snarling and wild, hurt and angry and Rio struggles not to grab her and take her in her arms, to tell her that it will be okay.

    Being the focus of the woman’s ire intimidates her in a way she didn’t expect, and Rio struggles to remember that it isn’t personal. She’s hurting, she’s lashing out, it’s not personal.

    It's hard.

    “If I called, would you answer?” She asks instead, surprising herself, and the gravity of her words drags her down. Threatens to drown her.

    “You could’ve texted,” Agatha snaps next. Rio runs her hand through her hair and nods.

    “Okay, Agatha,” she says quietly. “Go get some sleep. Eat. Shower. You don’t look like you’ve done any of that in days.”

    Agatha’s eyes have been glistening with tears since Rio walked in unannounced, and Agatha leaves before they can fall.

    “I need to feed Scratchy,” she murmurs as she goes. Spiting her by citing some other reason to leave.

    Rio collapses into the chair at Nicky’s bedside. Sunlight is glittering into the room, and it’s the first time in. . .has it been a year since she’s seen him during the day? Instead of always being by his side at night? The thought squeezes her heart tightly, but Rio doesn’t let it consume her.

    “It’s going to get better, baby,” she tells him, even though he’s asleep.

    Rio stands up and presses a kiss to his forehead.

    The machine stops beeping.

    Her heart stops with his.

    Doctors and nurses rush in, and Rio steps aside watching them handle her sweet little boy, struggling to breathe life back into him. Rio stands against the wall holding her breathe, unable to look away from him. This can’t. . .this can’t be it she was. . .she was trying to save him this can’t be it.

    “You have to save him,” she says aloud. Nobody hears her.

    “You have to save him, do something!” She screams, and the nurses realize she’s still there. Someone grabs her by the shoulders and tries to get her to leave, but Rio can’t look away from him. “You have to save him, there has to be something you can do!” She bellows, being pushing out into the hallway.

    The blinds are open, she sees with they take the defibrillator and charge it up. Sees the jerk of his chest as it shocks him. The heartlines jerk, and then fall flat. Two more times, she feels her hope rise and fall.

    The doctors all step back, despair on their faces. One of them storms out upset, but Rio is left standing in the hallway, tears leaking down her cheeks unkempt.

    Someone tells her that she can say goodbye, and Rio numbly walks into the room.

    Her tears splatter against his skin, and Rio bows over the bed and kisses his cheek once. Twice.

    “Wait for us, Nicky,” she whispers. Rio chokes on her own breathe and staggers backwards, pulling out her phone.

    Agatha. She has to tell Agatha. Oh god how is she going to tell her.

    Rio dials, doesn’t expect an answer, but when she hears her voice and tries to speak, her breathe hitches.

    “No,” Agatha moans. “No, please, no. This can’t be it.”

    She hears Agatha fall apart from miles away, and watches half of her life be taken away.

    Feels herself fall apart, even if she hides it far better than she expected.

    Rio tries to offer comfort. Touch. Conversation. Agatha shuts her out. Kicks her out of the house, barely gives her a chance to get clothes or personal affects, and then she’s forced out the door.

    On the porch they once laughed together at, Rio stands with all she has in her hands, and slowly takes a step away from the best years of her life.

    Pretends it doesn’t kill her to walk away, to do as Agatha asked.

    “That’s it?” Rio asks flatly. Her duffel bag is over one shoulder, heavy with all the clothes she could grab in the heat of Agatha’s grief.

    Standing in the doorway with her arms crossed, Agatha is glaring something furious at her. Rio is used to it by now. It hurt, at first, but after being poked by a prong one too many times, the pain tends to dull.

    “Yeah, Rio,” Agatha snaps. “That’s it.” She looks her up and down, shakes her head and looks away.

    Rio turns her head, hair covering the distraught expression she can’t hide in time. She takes a deep breath. She’ll do whatever Agatha needs her to, to make this easier, but this. . . this holds a finality to it that doesn’t sit right with her. It’s all wrong. Everything about this.

    Nicky shouldn’t be what ended up tearing them apart.

    “Agatha. . . “ Rio grounds herself by hooking her thumb into her belt, scraping her thumbnail against her hip. “What can I do? Is me leaving really what would fix this?” She asks, only able to keep her voice even for fear it would break. It comes out flat.

    “Don’t try to take it back now, it’s too late,” Agatha snarls, upper lip curled. Stepping out of the doorway, Agatha comes up to her, pressing her forefinger into her chest. “You left, Rio. You left and I hate you.” Her voice is iron. Unwavering.

    Unforgiving.

    “You never let me explain, Agatha,” Rio reasons, unflinchingly meeting her furious gaze.

    “I don’t care to hear your excuse,” Agatha sneers. She looks her up and down, disgust in her eyes, and steps back. “I’ll see you in therapy.” With a flick of her wrist, she spins and walks into the house, shutting the door sharply.

    In an office downtown, Rio does her best to hold it all together. To keep the anguish and pain and hurt inside. She doesn’t want to make this worse, but she doesn’t know how to voice to Agatha what she’s feeling. She’s tired of being pushed out. Pushed away.

    She doesn’t want to give up, Rio doesn’t think she’ll ever be able to give up Agatha Harkness, but there is a point a person can reach when it all hurts too much. There’s a job waiting for her, and her family. . .she doesn’t have a family, anymore. Again.

    Her family doesn’t want her anymore.

    The brief time she did enjoy the warmth of a family will stick with her for a long time, and she’ll always be grateful for it. Rio misses Nicky with everything she has, and it takes all her strength to keep going without him. Without Agatha, too. She’s hurting, it’s not her fault.

    Agatha will grieve in her own way, and Rio will help however she can. It’s not her fault. Rio can see her, has always seen Agatha for who she really is ever since that first night they met outside some corner store way past midnight. Seeing her for who she is means she knows what Agatha is going to do before the woman has decided to do it.

    Fixing her coat as they step out onto the sidewalk of the therapist’s office, Rio puts her sunglasses on to hide the heartbreak in her eyes. She knows it’s over. Done. Agatha has made up her mind and nothing Rio can do will change it until she’s ready. When she is, Rio hopes she’ll be there.

    “Te veo,” Rio says to her, tilting her chin up. Agatha side eyes her and nods stiffly, walking away at a clipped pace.

    Jingling her keys in her hands, Rio suffers herself one last moment, and watches her until she’s around the corner. She sighs heavily and looks around at the street, ringing the keys around her middle finger and then holding them in her hand. She’ll wait her whole life for Agatha Harkness.

    Rio stops coming back. She doesn’t want to stay away from her, but she has to. Agatha is grieving, and she’s lashing out. She doesn’t mean to, but Rio wishes Agatha would let her help, but the stubborn woman has made up her mind.

    So Rio will wait for her to change it.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Wanda is glaring at her every five seconds. Agatha sighs and lets her head rest back against the headrest. She can feel Wanda’s eyes even though she can’t see it. Agatha shifts, feels the car bustle over a bump.

    “I don’t want to hear your speech about family,” Agatha says flatly.

    “Good. Maybe you’ll listen to my speech about reason,” Wanda says sharply.

    Agatha groans and raises her head, wincing at the way her vision seems to drag and unfocused. She rubs lightly at her uninjured temple and looks over at her. Wanda is gripping the steering wheel tightly, glaring ahead of her.

    The second that the staff was able, Agatha signed her own release paperwork and left. There were countless protests, but they couldn’t make her stay, so she then coerced Wanda into driving her home – managing to do so by reminding Wanda that it’s Christmas and she should be with her family. Agatha’s clothes were ruined in the wreck, but someone – Rio, always so thoughtful Rio – had brought her bag from her mothers, rightfully assuming Agatha wouldn’t be wanting to go back.

    Without a word to anyone else, Agatha left in Wanda’s car and here they are several hours later, still stewing over the same argument. It had taken a long time to convince Wanda to take her home and not call Rio. Agatha is certain Wanda tried anyway, even though she’s notnentirely sure what Rio would’ve been able to do to her. Spouse by law doesn’t mean loss of agency.

    “Not really, no,” she murmurs.

    “Well-“

    “Head injuries and arguments don’t go well together,” Agatha interrupts, smiling snidely at her.

    “Then maybe you should have stayed in the hospital.” Touche.

    “What were they going to do for me?” Agatha sarcastically asks. “Monitor me? I’m fine.”

    “Oh, I didn’t realize your doctorate was in the medical field,” Wanda sasses her. Agatha glowers out the windshield. “Why do you keep pushing her away? You want to be with her, I know you do.”

    “I have my reasons,” Agatha mutters.

    Once she gets back to her office, tomorrow, maybe, she can sort it all out. There is much to unfuck in this whole situation, but Agatha is confident she can pull a few strings, call in a few favors, and be done with it all.

    Evanora Harkness messed with the wrong woman.

    “Is the desire to die alone one of them?” Wanda snaps. She pulls into a gas station and throws the car into park, facing Agatha. “I sincerely think it is. Why else are you constantly pushing the people that care about you away?” The severeness of her expression gives Agatha pause.

    Shifting in her seat, Agatha blinks a few times in surprise. Wanda has a similar expression to the one Rio wore when they climbed into a car and drove away from the mayoral mansion.

    Wanda hesitates, looks at her closely, and then sighs. She grabs the door handle and pauses.

    “Need anything from inside?”

    “Pack of smokes,” Agatha says. Wanda fixes her with a flat look and gets out without saying another word.

    The sound of the gas station are muffled with the door shut, and Agatha sighs heavily in her own silence. She leans her head back and looks out the window, dreading the attempt to fall asleep tonight.

    Since waking up in Rio’s arms all those days ago, she’s had a thousand and one things to distract her, but tonight she’s going to have to face her demons alone – most specifically, the demons that enjoyed being wrapped up in Rio. Agatha finds herself missing the feeling of belonging, and she searches for a way to assuage it that won’t sacrifice her dignity.

    She’s quietly searching for it up to the point that Wanda drops her off at home and Agatha is left well and truly alone in her house.

    That was a fight in and of itself.

    “It’s Christmas, Agatha, I don’t want to leave you alone for Christmas,”

    Wanda insists.

    Agatha curls her lip in disdain as she pulls her bag from Wanda’s car and walks up to her front door. She slings it over her shoulder and winces, grabbing at the gauze taped there. Glass had embedded into the soft flesh just inside the ball joint, narrowly avoiding her lungs by some grace of higher powers.

    "I've spent the last several Christmases alone, Wanda, this one will be no different,” Agatha says plainly. She’s merely stating a fact – and by this point in her life, it doesn’t bother her, anymore.

    “How about breaking that streak?” Wanda barters, following her into the house.

    “And spend it with the in-laws you so clearly love? I don’t think so,”

    Agatha chortles.

    Setting her bag on the kitchen island, Agatha glances around the house.

    Thankfully, it isn’t a mess, not that she had really expected it to be, but one never knows. It could still use a nice clean, though. Mail piled on the table. Dust everywhere. She needs to sweep and mop again, that’s certain.

    “I. . .can’t really argue that, can I?” Wanda murmurs, having been verbally cornered.

    Agatha shoots her a look and leans against the kitchen island. Honestly, all she wants is sleep, at this point. Her body and mind are both exhausted to the point that it’s a wonder she’s still standing.

    "I'm not losing out on anything, I promise, I need to sleep, anyway, I’d be poor company,” Agatha continues. Wanda narrows her eyes on her and raises her chin.

    “Fine, but I’ll keep my eyes on you, Harkness,” Wanda threatens.

    “Yeah, yeah, thanks for coming to get me,” Agatha says quickly, murmuring. Wanda smiles proudly at her and turns on her heel, promptly leaving Agatha alone for the first time in. . . in too many days she’s now alone.

    Listlessly, she drifts through the house. Feeds Senor Scratchy, puts the bourbon back that Wanda had taunted her with – that isn’t even opened, much to Agatha’s annoyance. Does laundry. Puts it away. Makes a sad excuse for a meal.

    Agatha can’t pretend that she’s fine, and she doesn’t have to anymore, now that she’s alone. It doesn’t mean she does anything but dwell on the complications in her life, but the ability to relax in peace, alone, at long last is not one she’s about to take for granted.

    Later that day, as she’s sitting on the couch watching whatever came on TV with Scratchy in her lap, there’s a knock on the door. Agatha knows only one person who would be on her front porch on today of all days and merely tips her head back and groans.

    “Agatha, I know you’re home, come open the door,” Wanda’s muffled voice snaps.

    Agatha glares at the general direction of the door, wondering how long the woman is willing to stand out there and if she can wait her out.

    “I know where your spare key is, so I’m either coming in my way or you can let me in,” Wanda warns.

    Groaning again, Agatha gingerly sets Scratchy onto the floor and makes her way to the door. She slings it open, already glaring, and Wanda is piled up in winter clothes and beaming at her with rosy cheeks, Tupperware that is still steaming in her hands.

    “What are you wearing?” Agatha wonders, taking in the puke brown of her sweat and the poorly stitched reindeer nose and antlers that are clearly stuffed and hanging by a thread.

    “A sweater, scooch, Harkness,” Wanda presses forward and Agatha grumbles as she steps aside. She shuts the door after her, glancing at the woman’s house down the street.

    The lights are on and she can see through the front window that two people are stood up, waving their arms dramatically. Ah, right. The in-laws. Wanda has been escaping to Agatha’s house in secret this whole time, Agatha should’ve expected the woman to come back even though she’s home.

    “What have they done this time, dare I ask?” Agatha drawls, sauntering after her once she closes the door.

    “Politics!” Wanda chimes from the kitchen.

    “Oh, by all means, help yourself,” Agatha murmurs as she leans against the archway of the kitchen, crossing her arms. Wanda is in her fridge with no preamble, bustling around within. She pops up from the door and grins.

    “Great!” Wanda pulls a bottle of wine out of her coat and sets it on the counter. “Boys are already asleep,” she says with a wink. “Glasses?” Wanda looks around as if she doesn’t already know where they are.

    “Please,” Agatha scoffs, but grabs two glasses anyway, sliding them over as Wanda opens the bottle. “They have to be suspicious of how often you’re leaving your own house.”

    “Well, considering I was house sitting and now have a friend who was just in a car accident, I have some valid reasons for leaving,” Wanda says. She looks pointedly at her as she pours a glass and slides it back to her. Agatha shrugs and looks away. “How are you feeling?”

    “Oh, just wonderful!” Agatha theatrically grins at her. “Like a million bucks.”

    Wanda rolls her eyes but smiles fondly at her. She looks around, taking a drink, and her eyebrows shoot up. Agatha clears her throat and avoids her eyes.

    “You. . .cleaned. . .” She notes warily, side eyeing her.

    “I had some free time,” Agatha shrugs.

    Under Wanda’s studious attention, Agatha fears the woman will sniff her out in a second. Sue her, she cleaned so she wouldn’t have to think about the last several days. So she could pretend everything was normal, that she still hates her wife with a passion that will never die. That she definitely is not crushed by the knowledge that said wife has someone else while Agatha is all alone.

    Thinking of all of it, Agatha purses her lips and takes a hefty drink.

    “Right.” Wanda, blessedly, doesn’t push the issue for once. “We don’t have to talk about it,” she offers quietly.

    In a rare moment of sincerity, Agatha shoots her a subdued but grateful smile. Wanda smiles softly back at her and Agatha clears her throat and walks away.

    “Well, I don’t suppose you have any grand ideas on how to pass the time?

    Jeopardy is on, and you know how thrilling that can be,” Agatha drawls.

    She takes her glass and starts walking back into the living room as someone wins a Double Jeopardy, Wanda trailing behind her, taking off some of her many layers as she goes.

    “Jeopardy can be fun,” Wanda says politely. Agatha groans under her breathe and glances at her as Wanda neatly tucks herself into one side of the couch and looks at the TV.

    “It’s kind of gross how nice you are, has anyone ever told you that?” She rhetorically asks, unable to keep the fondness from her voice. Wanda simply beams at her.

    “Thank you!” She accepts easily.

    Groaning again, Agatha collapses into the other side of the couch. They manage the rest of the episode in silence, and Agatha stews over why she’s here. What could possibly prompt her to come over here now? She looks at Wanda, again, trying to figure the woman out to no end.

    “What do you do?” She asks suddenly, and Wanda blinks out of a TV haze and looks over at her owlishly. “for work.”

    “Oh! I’m a kindergarten teacher,” Wanda smiles.

    “Ugh, of course you are,” Agatha rolls her eyes. She looks Wanda up and down, but the job fits a little too well. There’s something more to her that is familiar in a way she can’t place. “Have you always been?” She wonders, narrowing her eyes.

    “Oh, no,” Wanda laughs. She tilts her head, watching Agatha in amusement. “I was in the Special Forces before that. For a little while I was a mercenary, that’s how I met vision, actually! I was in Florida for a special detail and Vision was there at a cyber security conference. I ended up running into him more than once after my detail was over and the rest is history!” She’s beaming in a rather love-sick manner and Agatha stares at her in shock.

    “Well, that. . .makes sense,” she says with a shake of her head. Agatha narrows her eyes on her and stands up. “Refill?” She offers, holding her hand out for her glass already.

    “I’ll come with,” Wanda offers, pushing to her feet.

    They pad into the kitchen and Agatha watches her move, now noting the different things she does that had screamed at her in familiarity before, only now she can place it. The way she moves a little too fluid, her eyes scanning everything around her without thinking, always flicking to the exit, the windows. Her careful steps that are always quiet but keep her weight evenly displaced. The way her arms hang at her side, her hands too open, searching for the familiar weight of a gun at her hip.

    She moves like Rio does, just a little different.

    “Well, aren’t we all full of surprises today,” Agatha murmurs. She raises an eyebrow at Wanda and the woman looks over at her with an innocent tilt to her head. “Nothing. Here.” She slides her now full glass back over to her. “To holidays not spent with family,” Agatha sardonically says, raising her glass.

    Wanda laughs and clinks her glass together. “In-laws,” she corrects with a sigh.

    “Right, right,” Agatha nods placatingly.

    If she keeps a closer eye on Wanda after that little revelation, well, who can blame her?

    She goes through the next several days in a haze. Going into work is a blessing, and she pushes aside all the worries and concerns about the fresh wounds on her face – she has avoided looking at herself in the mirror as much as possible, she doesn’t want to see the pale bruises and remember the moments leading up to it. When she isn’t actively in a court room, she’s making calls and doing everything she can to sort out what she’s able.

    Agatha works tirelessly to undo the mess her mother put onto her, fielding document after document sent her way. She’s scanning through one when her phone rings, and she picks it up without looking as a messenger taps on her door and she waves him in.

    “Judge Harkness,” she answers.

    The messenger holds out a piece of paper to sign for receipt, and she glances it over before signing it. She frowns deeply at the words written in front of her as the person on the phone starts talking.

    “Agatha, it’s William. Um. William Kaplan. Uh . . . Jen could really use some help, I know you probably don’t want to be contacted. . . . but it has to do with-“

    Agatha finishes reading the receipt and looks up at the messenger in pure shock.

    “You’ve been served,” he says, unhanding the packet as she grabs the papers from him. “Sign, please.” He nods down at the paper and Agatha has little choice but to sign and hand it back.

    She knows exactly what lays within this folder, without looking she knows what lays within.

    “-your mother and the whole. . . thing. Uh. She’s packing up the house, I guess it’s been gone through already and she doesn’t know what else to do,” William goes on, unaware of what she’s dealing with.

    Agatha slowly opens the packet and reads through the first few lines. Her heart drops to the floor at the same time as her fury fills every inch of her being and ignites her into such a fuss that only years of self control keep her from flipping the desk in front of her on its side.

    “I’ll be in Salem by the end of the day,” Is all she snaps into the phone before slamming it into its holder.

    COMPLAINT OF DISSOLUTION sits big and bold at the top of the page, and Agatha feels fury bubble up thick and sweltering.

    Agatha storms into her home in a whirlwind of chaos. She slaps her mail on the counter and growls her frustration. The dark yellow folder sits at the bottom of the pile and Agatha snarls and screams her anger loud and wild.

    She doesn’t want to open it. Look at it. Touch it.

    Served. She’s been served. How fucking ironic. Agatha wants to tear the documents up and set them on fire, but she resists. She’s going to confront her about this.
