Agatha lays down to keep reading, slightly upwards of supine with most of the pillows behind her and small tablet in hand. Rio scoots across the expanse of the mattress and throws an arm around her stomach and then a leg over hers, pulling Agatha’s thigh into her greedily. Rio’s head ends up somewhere in the empty space the pillows create beneath the low of her back, her breathe casting upon her skin from the shirt that had ridden up.

    When Saturday comes, Agatha paces the floors of the cabin. Alone. She glares out at the backyard more than she wants to count. Tidies the area, just for something to do with her hands. Makes some tea. Attempts to sit down and keep reading her book. She keeps looking to the other side of the couch, and the emptiness of it grates on her. Agatha doesn’t mind it, really. She’s spent the last five years with Rio’s absence, what’s a day or two?

    But that day or two is everything, it seems. Agatha has grown accustomed to Rio’s presence, somehow, after such a short time of being allowed to indulge in her. It isn’t enough. It never will be. It’s too much, at the same time. How can she just. . .fall into this so easily? What’s wrong with her? Agatha still has grudges against Rio for how things ended. They haven’t discussed anything, and at the thought Agatha immediately shuts herself down.

    They haven’t had that talk, because Agatha is certain that once they do, they will never see each other again. Scars that have barely begun to heal ache and blister like fresh wounds and she can’t stand it. She had moved on. At least, she had tried to, and here she is acting as though all of this happened only yesterday. It isn’t fair, that Rio can do all of this to her after only a few days. It’s not.

    After the second time that she refolds the same blanket just for something to do, she scoffs to herself.

    Agatha Harkness is many things. An idle woman she is not. A spiraling woman, she is no longer.

    Planting herself at the kitchen table, she starts doing things she’d normally assign to an assistant. Or, hell, the work she’s doing is even something she’d normally leave the assistants to do just to keep them busy. Away from her.

    She’s bored.

    Agatha and boredom rarely mix. So, on the guise of getting out of the cabin she finds paperwork to file. Then she orders a car to come pick her up.

    It’s morning when she leaves, and well past dark when she returns.

    The day has left her frazzled and full of regrets about bad habits she should’ve shaken a long time ago. Things she should have the control to resist. Things she should not fall back on. To make mistakes over. Antics that she shouldn’t be engaging in. Juvenile things she’d long since thought she left behind.

    All of it is buzzing in her head as the paid driver pulls out of the driveway, the headlights flashing against the doorway as she swings the door open.

    She feels uneasy. Buzzing with wild energy she isn’t sure how to release and isn’t sure she knows how to.

    Rio is waiting, idly moving her phone around in her hands as she stands near the back of the couch. Her shoes are still on, and her coat is laid over the back of the couch. As Agatha enters the room, taking in the way she’s biting her lip and her hair is slightly frazzled, tied back in a loose ponytail but disheveled, as if she’d run her fingers through it a few times. At the sound of the door opening Rio whips her head towards her and slackens.

    She exhales quietly and begins to smile, opening her mouth to say something but she closes her mouth and then the distance between them in a few long strides.

    Agatha sighs and turns her head away, dismissively pawing at Rio’s hands that reach for her face. The woman is determined and grabs her chin, forcing Agatha to tilt her head just so. No doubt giving her full view of a rather nasty bruise and a split lip.

    “What the fuck happened?” Rio demands first, her eyes quickly burning with a ferocious, protective rage that only briefly manages to warm Agatha’s body. She quickly shoves Rio’s hands off her and steps away, shedding her coat first and then her shoes.

    “One of those hinge doors at the clerk’s office caught me in the lip, I went in to fax some things,” Agatha explains. For good measure, as she walks into the kitchen in search of tea, she tosses over her shoulder, “I was promised a fun weekend and instead spent it in a cabin by myself.” The dig is unwarranted, because Agatha knows why Rio is working so much, and she’s here to work, after all, but she can’t help herself. “Apparently, the daughter of the mayor who put her mother behind bars and then went against the town judge isn’t welcome there.”

    When Agatha glances over her shoulder, pulling out the tea kettle, Rio’s face stiffens for a moment as she looks away. Carding her fingers through her hair, Rio winces and pulls out the low ponytail her hair was in and then approaches Agatha slowly, analyzing her, but she’s distracted about it.

    Fidgeting with the ponytail in one hand, tying it up on her fingers in a way that distracts Agatha for only a moment before her racing mind yanks her away.

    “I’m sorry, it’s been. . .hectic, as I’m sure you can guess why,” Rio explains. She comes up beside Agatha, leaning her hip on the counter, tapping her nails on it idly, watching Agatha ready the water and then her mug shortly after. “Are you okay?” Her expression softens as she asks this, and the sight of that makes Agatha too warm and fuzzy.

    She isn’t in the state to reciprocate that sort of emotion, now.

    “Nothing I couldn’t handle,” Agatha waves aside. She sighs and stares at the tea kettle, literally watching water boil instead of facing Rio. “I hope you are getting some traction with all of this. Wouldn’t want her to weasel her way out of this too soon.”

    Agatha pulls the kettle off the stove before it can even whistle, pouring mildly warm water into her mug.

    With a sigh and another run of fingers through her hair, Rio leans so her back is against the counter, her torso twisted so she’s still facing Agatha, a hand on the counter. Those dark brown eyes begin to study her closely, watching her every move. Agatha is careful to keep her hands steady.

    “We’ve got her, I promise, Agatha,” Rio assures softly. “She isn’t getting away with this. With any of it.”

    Agatha dips and raises the tea bag out of the water a few times before having a drink while the bag is still in the mug. She makes a noise of complaint and glares down at it. Without hesitating, she dumps it into the sink, noting the wet tea bag sitting uselessly against metal. Agatha stares at the sight of the wet tea bag, slowly leaking water. It feels reflective of the emotions bumbling around in her chest, of what she wishes she could do with them; to just let them go, but she isn’t that type of person. Never has been and never will be. Agatha looks around for a better distraction, glazing over Rio quickly. Fixing the lay of her shirt collar and then her waistband, Agatha breathes in deep and meets Rio’s eyes.

    “Hungry?” She asks, and Rio blinks at her, thrown off guard. “Great, me too, let’s go get food.” Not leaving room for complaint, Agatha walks over to the couch and shoves Rio’s coat into her hands, quickly stepping back into her shoes and coat that she’d only just removed.

    “What?” Rio exasperates, slipping into her coat.

    “We have barely seen each other for the last two days, let’s get out of the cabin and. . . “ Agatha shrugs into her coat and then levels Rio with a look she hopes conveys something nonchalant, “socialize. Or something.”

    Agatha flaps her hand and pulls the door open.

    “You. . .you want to go into town? What is going on with you?” Rio asks, but follows her outside nonetheless, keys in hand. She clicks the FOB and the car lights illuminate their path forward. “I thought you just said the town doesn’t like you.”

    “The judges office doesn’t like me,” she corrects sharply. “Besides I’m tired of being in this cabin,” Agatha says dismissively, glaring up at the gentle snowfall as she shoves her hands into her pockets. Her fingers brush against the fresh pack and lighter in her pocket and they twitch with the urge. “And this is my last night with you, so. . . “ Agatha shrugs, passing a look over her shoulder. It’s the best deflection she can muster, but it eventually works its magic.

    Rio has her eyes narrowed on her, her lips in a flat line as she toys with the keys. The look she’s giving makes Agatha feel as though she’s in an interrogation room with Rio leant back against the wall, arms crossed and standing shoulder width apart, lamp light glaring into her eyes. She feels too scrutinized.

    She needs to get Rio drunk so she stops looking at her like that.

    “Right,” Rio says carefully. She pauses in her gait, hesitating for just a moment, that observative look lingering for just long enough to make Agatha think she’s about to start squirming, that Rio is going to confront her about all of this or that she somehow knows about what happened to Agatha today, but then she breathes in and the moment fades. “What time do you want to leave tomorrow?” Rio redirects easily, all traces of investigation gone.

    “That’s up to you,” Agatha says. “What time do you want to be back here?”

    They slide into the car and Rio makes herself comfortable in the driver’s seat before taking off. The car is still holding onto a shred of warmth. Rio must have arrived not long before Agatha. That thought, at least, assuages some of her guilt for being gone so long. It wasn’t exactly planned.

    “Well, I’ve worked a lot of overtime and HR isn’t happy with me,” Rio says casually. She twists, bracing her hand on Agatha’s seat as she turns around, no matter if the car is equipped with a backup camera. When she’s done backing up, Rio looks at her, her hand still on the back of her seat. “Maybe I could stay a day.”

    That makes Agatha forget everything running through her mind. She opens and closes her mouth, looking at Rio with unmasked surprise. How bold.

    How presumptuous. How perfect. Agatha allows herself to picture Rio in her home, pictures what it would be like to undress her on the way to the bedroom, to use every weapon in her arsenal on her and hear the satisfaction of every piece hitting just perfectly.

    Memories intertwine with the daydream, and the fact makes itself known that she’s already done all of those things. A pain sidles next to the daydream, next to the memories.

    Agatha really needs a drink.

    “If you want, I mean. . . whatever happens,” Agatha shrugs flippantly, murmuring as if the thought doesn’t stir both pain and a heady thrum of desire at once.

    Rio doesn’t reply, she faces forward and takes them into town. They comment on idle things; the latest break in the news, the condition of the roads, the impending winter storm and the effects it’s already had in town. Around the time they pull up to a parking spot downtown, Rio mentions Wanda and her hellions again and Agatha is already standing up out of the car when she says it, but she stops and waits for Rio to round the car, looking up at her with a sincere expression as she pockets the keys, before shutting the door sharply and pinning her with a sharp look that stops Rio in her tracks.

    “What?” Rio shrugs.

    “What is with you two? I want an actual answer this time,” Agatha growls.

    “We. . .worked together once upon a time,” Rio says lightly. She steps forward and Agatha begrudgingly follows her. Rio opens the door for her and Agatha glares at her as she walks past her. “Okay, fine. I was on a case, a long time ago, somewhere down in Florida for some. . .it doesn’t even matter. We ended up working with some government muscle and she was the team leader. We worked together on the case. That’s all.”

    Agatha narrows her eyes, flattens her lips, and hums disapprovingly. Rio flounders under her look and they step up to the hostess’s podium. It stands empty so Agatha faces her.

    “Worked on a case? Is that all?” Agatha drawls. Rio sighs heavily and looks away. “Or did you find solace in the only other government agent as boring as you?”

    “Jesus, Agatha, it wasn’t like that,” Rio cried in exasperation. Hands in her pockets, she spreads them in a placating gesture. “What is with you tonight?”

    “Nothing,” Agatha shrugged. Rio aims a look her way and Agatha shrugs again, tossing hair over her shoulder and looking around. “Nothing,” she said again, adding an innocent flare to her shoulder. Rio sighs and shakes her head, scanning the room for the hostess, her shoulders relaxing.

    Watching the shadow of her jaw and the flex of the muscle there, Agatha’s mind wanders to how many other people have found out about the way Rio’s knees go weak when kissed under her ear. “I just think it’s funny that you never mentioned her.”

    Rio groans, loudly, and rolls her neck. She faces Agatha, a slight stomp in her heel as she plants it.

    “I have never slept with her, I swear on my. . .” Rio looks around in thought and then lands her eyes on Agatha again, “I swear on my collection of vintage records that I never slept with your neighbor.”

    Agatha purses her lips, narrowing her eyes at Rio. The confession seems sincere, but Agatha feels something still bristling under the surface of her skin. She can’t tell if it’s from something Rio said or if it’s an issue of her own. Breathing deep, Agatha prepares to wheedle more information out of her and Rio already begins rolling her eyes in annoyance, clearly anticipating her next move.

    “Agatha!” The voice pierces through her train of thought and Agatha whips around, seeing none other than Lilia Calderu walking towards them, clearly in the middle of a meal considering she didn’t wear a coat as she approached. Her clear eyes pass to Rio. “Rio! How nice to run into you two again!”

    “Fucking thrilling,” Agatha mumbles, looking away as she crosses an arm over her stomach. She leans her elbow on the back of her hand, brushing hair from her face.

    “Hi, Lilia, how are you?” Rio easily engages her.

    “Just fine, taking my last dinner in town before the storm hits,” Lilie said, smiling politely at the two of them. The tension in the air from Agatha and Rio’s brief argument is obviously felt even by the sudden interruption, and Lilia looks from them to the still empty podium for something better to do. “Are you two waiting for a table?”

    “Yes,” Agatha said, at the same time as Rio replies with:

    “It looks pretty busy, but yeah.”

    “Well, you two can come join me if you like.” The offer is paired with a gentle smile that looks too knowingly at Agatha. That knowing look makes her stand up straight and glower.

    “We can wait.”

    “That would be great.”

    Rio shoots Agatha with a warning look that has Agatha tossing a hand up in the air and looking away. Rio then takes a step towards Lilia. Sighing in annoyance, Agatha shakes her head, pursing her lips.

    “Perfect, I just got in, over here.” Lilia walks away and Agatha digs her heels in to stay right here for the hostess rather than sit with a therapist, but Rio grabs her upper arm tightly and drags her along.

    Indignantly, Agatha scoffs, silenced by the severe look shot over Rio’s shoulder. She grumbles to herself and trudges along after her, both of them shedding their coats and draping them on their chairs as they settle into the table across from Lilia. A wine glass is already sitting waiting for her, but with a wave of Lilia’s hand the waiter comes back and takes their drink orders.

    When he’s gone, the three of them sit there staring at each other in a silence that makes Agatha glare around the restaurant.

    “So, how are you doing with your mother on trial?” Lilia asks. No time for pleasantries tonight, it seems.

    “She’s getting what she deserves,” Agatha says, not bothering to hide even a hint of bitterness from her voice. She’s toying with a strand of hair, pointedly not looking at either of her companions.

    How long can she sit here before she can find some way to sneak off to the alley and smoke? Surely a solid few minutes of conversation will be enough. Any less and she’d draw too much attention from either of them.

    Agatha needs to be patient enough, tonight, to not draw attention to herself. Something that is inherently difficult for her to do.

    “How’s business been?” Rio diverts, shifting at Agatha’s left.

    “Good, good,” Lilia replies, her voice thick. Agatha glances over with a frown, watching her set her wine glass down. The silent question is answered and Agatha scans the restaurant again. “I own a shop down in Westview, I’m afraid retirement is more boring than I anticipated.”

    That, somehow, doesn’t make Agatha relax even a little bit.

    “What kind of shop is it?”

    Rio indulges Lilia in small talk, and Agatha barely listens. Somewhere around the time Lilia mentions a legging line she stops even pretending to listen. Where the fuck is the waiter with her drink?

    “Agatha, I heard your mother posted bail today.”

    As she turns her head, she can hear in the back of her mind a very specific sound.

    Once, when she was a youth, she found her way to the roof of a church.

    Likely to hide from her mother and smoke a cigarette she’d coerced an older kid into buying for her. At the edges of the roof were old stone statues. One of them had gotten loose and Agatha had experimentally moved it around. The sound of stone grating on stone made her shiver and push harder if only to get the sound over with. That sound of stone grating is what she hears as she turns her head to look at Lilia, pinning her with a narrowed gaze. Lilia, far too comfortable and knowing, stares back with her wine glass in hand. Agatha drops the strand of hair she was toying with and faces Lilia.

    “Well, even the devil breaks free from time to time,” she says casually.

    Lilia raises her chin, and Agatha realizes something that chills her to the bone. Somehow. Some way, Lilia knows what happened today. Agatha can’t even begin to theorize how, but that doesn’t matter right now. What matters is that Rio doesn’t find out.

    “I still can’t believe that it fell through for us to hold her,” Rio comments. Side-eyeing her, Agatha can’t tell if she’s obtusely ignoring her and Lilia’s stare down, or if she’s truly unaware of it. “I thought it was pretty concrete.”

    “I hear there was quite an event at her release,” Lilia continues.

    Agatha is going to kill her. She’s worked on plenty of cases over the years. She’ll figure out the problems about what muscle it would take, or if she can even do it alone. One way or another Agatha will make sure Lilia is never found again.

    “Was there?” Agatha innocently asks. She smiles snidely. “Everything with Evanora turns into a whole event. I can’t say I’m surprised.”

    Lilia smiles flatly, those eyes pegging her instantly.

    “Oh, I think it’s just in the Harkness bloodline, wouldn’t you agree?”

    Whatever Lilia gets out of this, Agatha will make sure she regrets it, because whatever act Rio was feigning before it is dropped. She pulls her gaze from the TV above the bar – ah, it was some sporting event, so she wasn’t being obtuse, she was just distracted - and looks at Lilia with a frown, her mind moving a thousand miles a minute behind her eyes. Then she turns to Agatha. Something locks into place and Agatha groans, leaning back as Rio narrows her eyes, shifting to face her.

    “What happened?” She snaps. Rio’s eyes flicker to Agatha’s lip and cheek before Agatha can turn away and Rio’s look becomes thunderous.

    “Agatha,” she says warningly.

    “I don’t know what happened,” Agatha shrugs lightly. She glares at Lillia. “Because I wasn’t there,” she grinds out.

    “You weren’t where?” Rio demands, and when Agatha stays mum, Rio shifts to Lilia. “Do you know what happened?”

    “I heard about a scuffle outside the detention center. Nothing scandalous,” Lilia shrugs. Agatha relaxes impartially.

    Lilia shifts to sit upright, still holding her wine glass aloft.

    “Just that blows were exchanged out of camera view.”

    “Agatha!”

    “Where the fuck is the waiter?” Agatha growls, pushing up out of her seat to head straight for the bar.

    Rio pulls her back down and stares at her with a look she’s never been under the thumb of. It’s no doubt the look Rio gives unruly people she’s working with or against, and Agatha feels some sympathy for them, under the full weight of that gaze. It makes her want to shrink and say everything that she’s hiding, and it grates on Agatha to no end. She hates feeling like that, and it being Rio who looks at her like that makes the look so much more impactful.

    “Did she do this?” Rio demands, her eyes flicking to the split lip.

    Agatha opens and closes her mouth a few times.

    She’s been an attorney for well over a decade. A judge for at least three years. Paralegal long before any of that ever happened for her. Agatha has more than enough experience in lying and coming up with solutions on the spot. Hell, her quick thinking has gotten her out of too many hairy deals, but now as she stares at Rio, not a single thought comes to mind.

    She is absolutely stupefied, and the only thing that manages to come to mind is far more incriminating than she would like.

    “I gave as good as I got,” she says flatly. When Rio’s eyes widen, Agatha tosses a hand in the air and shrugs, looking away.

    “Agatha!” Rio exasperates, leaning her head back, sliding her hand down her face. Agatha allows her all the time she needs to collect herself, looking anywhere but at her or at Lilia. After a few moments Rio sighs heavily and looks at her. “Tell me everything.”

    “What?” Agatha scoffs out a laugh and her shoulders creep up to her ears. “There’s nothing to tell. She tripped. I tripped. Clumsy people, my family.” Agatha blows air out from between her tight lips and avoids Rio’s eyes.

    “I swear,” Rio growls, “If this gets dropped because you assaulted her – “

    “I just finished it I didn’t start anything –“

    “ – this is not what we need right now –“

    “ – go after her not me. I’m the innocent one!”

    “ – she can get away with technicalities you know how she works – “

    “ – and I have the legal defense council comparable to Zeus when it comes to child support –“

    “ – her legal team is impeccable you know that – “

    “ – because I trained them. I’m just a victim of self-defense –“

    “Here are you drinks.”

    Agatha takes her glass and throws the whole thing back, setting it back on the tray before the waiter can finish his sentence. He doesn’t miss a beat, handing Rio her drink and then looking at Agatha who waves for him to refill.

    “Come right back here with that drink and make it a double,” Agatha demands.

    “You assaulted her right out of the detention center,” Rio summarizes flatly, ignoring her drink.

    “No,” Agatha drawls at length. She tongues her teeth and shrugs. “She assaulted me. I’m serious about the self-defense angle.”

    “Agatha, you should have told me right away!” Rio says and her voice is thunderous.

    “Why?” Agatha scoffs. The waiter, thankfully, is already on his way back to her. “So you can include it in your case? Rio, I agree that it’s important, but this was an off the books situation. Surely you can appreciate something like that.”

    Agatha is only staring at the waiter as he approaches, and when the table falls silent and Lilia excuses herself, she feels as though she missed something. Frowning, she watches Lilia go and then looks over at Rio. The woman has an arm thrown over the back of her chair and bent so her fist is right in front of her chest, her knuckles white. Rio looks furious, and Agatha braces herself for that fury being focused on her. She’ll handle it. Whatever comes she’ll face it. She’s been through worse.

    “No,” Rio says simply. The waiter sets Agatha’s drink down and she reaches for it, pulling it up to her lips to avoid looking at Rio. “But because I would’ve risked everything to be the one that got even with Evanora in that way.”

    Agatha swallows thickly. Staring at the wall across from her, she keeps the glass pressed to her lips.

    Rio has a way of speaking about things when it comes to rules. A long time ago, Agatha could speak her language as if it were her own, could discern what she really meant as she vented about work in that careful way of hers and understand her perfectly fine. Like second nature.

    So, at the veiled words, Agatha knows exactly what Rio’s implying, and she slowly sets her glass down. Agatha hesitates before looking over at her with a raised eyebrow and a flattened mouth. Rio leans in, holding her gaze.

    “Nothing would satisfy me more than knocking her right across the jaw,”

    Rio elaborates, putting everything into plain language. Rio lets the moment hold for long enough that when she sits up straight and her eyes harden the words she says next render her completely speechless. “Deny me the privilege of that again, Agatha, and we’ll have more than just a short argument.”

    Rio stands up and disappears, leaving her coat behind which reassures Agatha that she’ll be back, but does nothing to help her center herself in the wake of those plainly stated words. Agatha is still holding her glass in the air, staring at the corner Rio disappeared around when the waiter comes back with a refill without being asked.

    Chapter End Notes Y'all long time commenters are the life of me. Thank you so much <3.

    Back to our regularly scheduled word count today. I've been stewing on this idea for a while now and trying to decide how to go about it, but today I just sat down and this is what came out and it's something that I'm finally happy with. I hope y'all like it!

    That's A Kindness You Can't Afford Chapter Summary Agatha and Rio talk through some of their problems.

    Chapter Notes Here we go at last, the talk.

    Some light angst, but not bad.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes

    Agatha finishes the second drink by the time Rio returns. Granted, it doesnt take long for her to finish the drink considering she’s acting like they’re water. Though she thought she’d nipped this bad habit – among others – in the bud a long time ago. Falling back into them isn’t exactly what she wants to do, but. . . “when in Rome” as they say.

    A refill is placed by the waiter rather quickly after finishing the second. As he passes by her, Agatha tracks him, her attention snapping to the shadow emerging from around the corner.

    Rio holds something in her hand, looking down at it as she exits the hallway on the other side of the wall. The focused anger that flattened her features as she left has smoothed out, but only a little. That gives Agatha some relief, though she’s still on edge. Agatha had expected more, despite the fact that Rio is typically less expressive. She expected the woman to be upset with her for any number of things, being jealous of what she’s done -

    Implied or not – is not what she ever would have guessed the woman to be upset over.

    The kind of anger she expected, Agatha was willing to sit through it, to deal with it, but this? This.. .anger on her behalf? It throws her for a loop. Rio is a naturally protective person, and Agatha knew that intimately once upon a time. A handful of nights out at bars ended with Agatha needlessly tending to bloody knuckles that were earned in her defense – because Agatha just can’t resist instigating when the opportunity presents itself.

    Never in a thousand years did Agatha expect Rio to still extend that attention to her. Before Rio, Agatha protected herself, and after her she did the same. It was that time in between that really sent her into a tailspin. That made it harder to accept it, now, because Agatha forgot what it felt like to be cared for in that way. Now it comes rushing back and fills her with a warmth and affection she wants to ignore because it’s going away before long.

    They can pretend in limbo, but once they have that conversation Agatha knows it’s over. Its inevitable.

    When Rio sits down, Agatha isn’t looking at her. She’s holding the refreshed drink aloft, swirling the ice in the glass. Gentle fingers grab her jaw tenderly and pull her aside, and had the hand not been caressing her so carefully, Agatha would’ve resisted.

    She wants to pull away roughly and snap at her to leave her be, but instead she melts into the touch. Agatha craves that kind of simple touch like she never has before. It does everything she wishes it didn’t and disarms every defense mechanism she has. All of her instincts drop at the touch of Rios lightly calloused fingers that hold her like she’s precious.

    Agatha lets herself be turned to face Rio and the woman’s expression is sharp with focus, but her eyes are full of concern. As carefully as she can, Rio dabs a paper towel against her lip. After a few moments Rio shifts her hand and pulls Agatha’s bottom lip down with her thumb. The paper towel comes away spackled red with blood and Agatha exhales gently, looking up into Rios eyes. Fully focused in her task, Rio doesn’t look up to meet her eyes until the paper towel is pulled away clean, and she keeps her hand on Agatha’s jaw as she does finally look up.

    “I’ll ask again; are you okay?” Rio echoes from earlier, swallowing tightly and as she does some of the severity leaves her faces, replaced with a kindness Agatha doesn’t know how to react to.

    “Fine, if you’d stop manhandling me,” Agatha splutters.

    Lightly rolling her eyes, Rio allows Agatha to slip out of her grip. Her hand hovers in the air for a moment before she pulls it back, bunching the paper towel up in her hand so no blood is exposed and then setting it down.

    Agatha shifts in her chair uncomfortably, glancing around the restaurant.

    “Can I get the real story?” Rio wonders. Though she doesn’t look at her as though Agatha has an option. She voices it like a question, but it isn’t really and it irks Agatha; that expectation. That same investigators stare is affixes on her and Agatha bristles at it.

    With a huff and a small smirk, Agatha let’s the question linger. She’s positive that if she tells Rio the full story – the true story, not what she’s made up - that it will be Rio behind bars by the nights end, not Evanora who rightfully belongs there. Though, she’d love nothing more than to see that fallout. It’s been awhile since she’s seen Rios right hook.

    Sliding her tongue over her teeth, Agatha drags her attention to Rio who’s watching her closely.

    “For many number of reasons; no,” she says. Rios brows pull down over her eyes, a frustrated question in them. “Plausible deniability for one reason,” Agatha explains with a flap of her hand, “and I don’t feel like going to bail you out of jail. Not tonight.” Any other night and she’d be happy to, but she’s still tender from the day she’s had.

    Throwing back amber and letting the burn of it slink down her throat, Agatha sighs, still clinking the ice around idly. She’s surprised that Lilia picked this place to randomly eat at. A horrible mix between a sports bar and a normal sit down eatery, TVs line the walls or hang from them.

    Random sports paraphernalia around the walls. The lighting is dim and it’s either to set an air of intimacy or to hide the dirty floors and poor excuse for food she sees at tables around them. At least there are no neon lights.

    “We’re going to a coffee shop, I’ll buy you a cup,” Rio snaps over her shoulder, shutting the door behind her.

    “Testy,” Agatha drawls to herself.

    Still grumbling under her breath, Agatha takes her sweet time getting her shoes on and putting on her coat. She pauses at the mirror by the door and checks over herself. Part of her wants Rios patience to break and make her storm back into the cabin full of fury. Agatha doesn’t know what she’d get out of it, but she kind of wants to see it.

    Witnessing Rios emotions, unbridled and naked, is a thrill she did not anticipate she’d enjoy.

    To her chagrin, Rio doesn’t come get her. Agatha locks the door from the inside and then pulls it shut, turning back to check that it’s locked. Sliding her hands into her pockets and determined to just stroll over to the car, she stops short, and her jaw drops in shock.

    Leant back on the hood of the car, long coat draped just so, foot propped on the grill, shades over her eyes and looking positively devourable, Rio is smoking a cigarette.

    Agatha starts sauntering over to her with a smile, snow crunching underfoot with every step. “Well, if I’d have known you were doing that I would’ve –“

    “No time. Get in.” Rio flicks the cigarette to the ground and steps on it, pushing off the car and rounding it quickly. Ripping the door open, she slides into the seat.

    Shocked again, Agatha stops in her tracks. She’s staring at Rio through the windshield and Rio is staring back. When Agatha doesn’t make a motion to move, Rio revs the car.

    “Oh. My. God. Can you relax?” Agatha drawls, sliding into the seat.

    Rio twists around and looks behind them as she backs up. Shaking her head, Agatha pulls out her own sunglasses and sets them in her nose. It’s annoyingly bright and sunny for a day in early January.

    “Did you grab your charger? Phone?” Rio checks, but she doesn’t show any sign that she’d actually go back if Agatha said no.

    “Yes, dad,” Agatha chides. Agatha brushes hair from her face with her forefinger and Rio’s jaw flexes.

    Rio flattens her lips and looks at her. Agatha raises her eyebrows, leaning her head back partially. Smoothly, Rio faces forward and starts driving.

    “My god, have you always been like this about road trips?” Agatha wonders teasingly, a slight smile curving her lips.

    “Do you want the ride or not?” Rio snaps.

    “Oooh, you sound mad,” Agatha taunts.

    “Traffic is going to be hell. We and everyone in the tristate area are all on the road going home today and I do not want to fuck around,” Rio says, gripping the wheel tighter. “I don’t want to spend more time than necessary on the road. I’ve checked the maps, and we should make it in under four and a half hours if we don’t stop at all.”

    The fact that Rio has so easily called their destination home warms Agatha’s heart.

    “Check the maps?” Agatha splutters and then laughs. “Did you look at an actual atlas or do you mean your phone?”

    Warm and fuzzies does not mean Agatha will lessen her teasing.

    “I don’t trust the Internet for directions, Agatha,” she drawls. “They mislead you in order to make you waste more gas, so you stop at the right gas stations and then while you’re there you get food and drinks and it’s a whole ploy to get you to give up more money.”

    “And why are you so stingy about money?” Agatha probes.

    The full focus of Rios expression breaks for less than half a second. The piercing stare out the window falters and her eyes glisten. The white knuckled grip on the steering wheel slackens. Agatha remembers why Rio has the job she does, and she realizes why she should’ve left the subject alone. Guilt sweeps in and batters her ribcage that feebly protects her heart from it.

    “It’s not about money it’s about the big corporations’ greedy hands grabbing for more money, money that they don’t even need, it’s ridiculous,” Rio snarls. The moment is gone, and the focused, frustrating Road Trip Captain returns easily, as if nothing happened.

    “Woo!” Agatha huffs with a smile, taking it in stride. “You’re all fired up. I’ll keep that in mind next time I want you to get a little rough.” A wicked smile curves her lips as Rio grumbles Under her breath in complaint. Ready to lay it on thick, Agatha falls into an act. “Ooh, harder, baby, I’m a big corporation trying to steal your time and money,” Agatha mocks, pursing her lips and altering her voice to a seductive but mocking drawl.

    “You’re. On. Thin. Ice.”

    Though the words are sharp and harsh, Agatha can see the gleam of amusement in her eyes and settles into her seat. She stays quiet for the rest of the drive, watching the trees and then the buildings pass by as they enter town. They find a parking spot downtown, with a new argument.

    “No, over there, you’re not – Rio if you go there you have to –“

    “It’s a Sunday you don’t pay for street parking on Sunday, Agatha,” Rio snarls.

    “What is going on with you?” Agatha asks, raising her eyebrows, her voice rising in pitch. Rio glances at her and Parks the car on the side of the street, turning it off and shoving the door open.

    Fondly rolling her eyes Agatha steps out onto the sidewalk and falls into step beside Rio. The woman fiddles with the keys before shoving them into her pocket, and then she squints around the storefronts.

    “Which shop?”

    “There’s one, Rio,” Agatha murmurs in amusement.

    Rios nostrils flare, and behind those tinted shades she furiously stares at her. Though she doesn’t say anything, Rios shoulders are tense and she’s practically stomping her feet as they walk down the sidewalk.

    The amusement keeps her warm all the way to the coffee shop. Agatha spots Jen and William standing in line and walks up to them, staring up at the menu and seamlessly entering their conversation.

    “– it was too sugary even for me, I wouldn’t get it,” William says flatly, a slightly wrinkle to his upper lip.

    “I like my sugar but if you say it’s too much. . .” Jen trails off. She tilts her head to the side contemplatively. “What time are you two hitting the road?” Jen asks her, still looking up at the menu.

    “Right after this,” she murmurs. “Captain Itinerary has us on a strict schedule,” Agatha laughs. She shoots a glare behind her towards Rio who looks at her back, staring up at the menu and ignoring Agatha.

    “Captain Itinerary? Inventive,” Jen chides.

    Agatha mocks her voice and rolls her eyes, but both she and Jen are hiding smiles. Crossing her arms, Agatha leans towards Jen, opening her mouth to make a sly comment.

    “The line has moved.” Hands grasp her waist and manhandle her forward.

    To her offense, Rio actually picks her up off the ground and moves her forward.

    Offended, Agatha twists around to glare at Rio who is already glaring ahead, arms back to her sides.

    “You can’t just move me around like that,” Agatha blusters.

    “I can,” Rio drawls. She looks down at Agatha, sunglasses perched on her head, brown eyes on full, knee wobbling display. “You’re very small.”

    “I am not small!” Agatha gasps in indignation. Jen smiles flatly, struggling to hide her amusement.

    “You are,” Jen confirms. “You have small man syndrome.”

    “What?!” Agatha growls, fully facing her. “Take that back.”

    “No, it’s true,” Jen defends, raising one of her crossed arms up in the air. “You, Agatha, have tiny dick syndrome. Most short people do, honestly.”

    “I’m average,” Agatha points out flatly.

    Rio towers just slightly over her on account of the heeled boots she wears. Despite not working, she’s dressed as always in a cleanly pressed suit and those stupid heeled boots. Agatha forms a comment in her mind about them and how she and Rio are actually the same height.

    “Congratulations!” William chimes, smiling good-naturedly at her, absolving her of the chance to make the comment.

    “Give yourself some credit, baby,” Rio murmurs close to her. Agatha whips her head up – cautious of the low purr of her voice that makes her body thrum in excitement - as Rio edges past her towards the counter, her hand at her hip. “You’re bigger than average, just not taller.”

    Jen bursts into laughter, overhearing the quiet comment easily. She rears back and then leans over, holding her stomach. Agatha sneers at her as Rio starts ordering. Rather childishly – and only taking a moment to debate her actions – Agatha flicks Jens ear. The woman straightens up and narrows her eyes, still smiling, and Agatha darts away, partially hiding behind Rio.

    “Oh, I see, use the fed to protect you, uh-huh,” Jen rolls her eyes.

    “Come get me,” Agatha challenges. “Unless you’re scared,” Agatha teases rather victoriously.

    “Coffee will be up soon,” Rio says, stepping away. “I’m going to the bathroom. You better, too, before we leave, I’m serious about not stopping.” And then Rio promptly abandons her to her sisters rage.

    “What, now?” Jen gloats, stepping up to the counter and incidentally beside Agatha, who hasn’t moved since Rio’s departure.

    “Oh, shove it,” Agatha murmurs. She moves out of the way, finding a quiet corner to sink into with a window overlooking the sidewalk.

    Though she’s been ignoring it since they walked in, Agatha is more aware of the states their little group is receiving now that she’s separated from them. She knows it’s not just because they might’ve been too loud and too childish for their age. It’s their family problems that draw the disappointed glares and stares of the customers.

    Agatha sneers at any who meets her eyes, leaning back in her seat and crossing one leg over the other, completely unaffected by them. Jen and William slide into seats across from her, and Agatha keeps glaring around until nobody is looking at them.

    “So, how was mother dearest upon her return?” Agatha asks quietly, thankful nobody is near them.

    “Don’t know,” Jen shrugs. “She didn’t come to the manor after she was bailed out.”

    “Hm. I’m sure she’s in for a real surprise once she sees you’ve packed up her house,” Agatha murmurs. She glances around and then frowns. “Why did you pack everything up?”

    William blushes in embarrassment and looks out the window beside them.

    Jen shifts and then sighs.

    “We thought she was in jail indefinitely and that the house would get possessed by the government or something,” William rushed out quietly.

    A moment of silence passes around the table and in the wake of the admission Agatha throws her head back cackling.

    “Not everyone knows how this works, Agatha,” Jen snaps.

    “You thought it was all going to get taken?” Agatha asks rhetorically. Jen rolls her eyes and Agatha laughs again, the sound cut shirt as Jen kicks her under the table. “Hey!”

    Agatha leans down to rub at her shin where sharp pain shoots up and down it. Straightening up, Jen looks over and then her expression turns mockingly serious when she looks at Agatha again. Following her line of sight, Agatha spots Rio approaching their table.

    “Be mature,” Jen says pointedly.

    Rio arrives at the table, two coffees in hand. The rest of them struggle to not laugh at the clear dynamic going on. Rio; the stern adult figure, and the rest of them; immature children.

    “Thanks,” Agatha murmurs, stifling a laugh as she takes her coffee. Rio sits back in her chair, getting comfortable.

    Jen and Agatha lock eyes and struggle contain their laughter. Agatha makes the mistake of looking at William who’s hiding his face behind his hand.

    Rio remains unaware of what’s going on.

    “What time are you two supposed to get to Westview?” Jen wonders.

    The question is innocent enough, and its decent small talk. Agatha for some reason finds it hilarious and has to stifle a giggle. She shifts and clears her throat, earning a confused side glance from Rio.

    “Four thirty if we aren’t held up,” Rio says. “It could’ve been three thirty if we were to leave when we were supposed to.” The pointed dig at Agatha taking forever to get ready makes Agatha turn her head away and laugh, struggling to hide it with a cough.

    “Stick-in-the-mud,” she coughs into her hand.

    Jen giggles and quickly stifles it. Smiling, Agatha glances over at her, catching sight of William, bowing over as his shoulders shake roughly. Rio calmly finishes getting settled and leans back in her seat, taking a casual drink out of the cup.

    “If you’re making fun of me, I’ll remind you that we’re about to be stuck in a small space for several hours,” Rio says flatly. Agatha raises her head and faces her. “I will make you miserable.”

    “Nice going, Jen,” Agatha chides, kicking her shin. Jen jolts the table, and Agatha throws her head back laughing, only to feel something smack into her chest. She winces and glares at Jen as she catches the coaster in her hand. “Real mature.”

    “You started it,” Jen says, raising her eyebrows.

    William laughs harder in the corner, wheezing under his breath.

    Agatha mocks Jens voice again, and the woman narrows her eyes, her lips quirking up.

    “How old are you?”

    “Older than you,” Agatha scoffs, recrossing her legs. “Respect your elders,” she says seriously.

    “At least she doesn’t need glasses to read,” William wheezes.

    “What did I just say?” Agatha asks in offense. Jen high-fives William and the two of them giggle in victory.

    “Can you please behave?” Rio sighs tiredly.

    “Fine, fine,” Agatha laments. She shrugs and shifts in her seat, leaning back into the chair.

    The table settles, all of them aimlessly looking around. After a few moments Jen leaves, returning with hers and William’s coffee. They drink in silence, watching people walk past the window.

    “Did you hear about what happened last night?” Jen wonders.

    Having calmed down and “matured” the table all look to Jen. She glances at each of them curiously, hesitating on Rio.

    “We saw a news report about an assault outside the detention center, is that what you mean?” Rio asks, something glittering in her eyes.

    “Do you know anything else about it?” Jen leans forward partially.

    “Nothing I can tell you,” Rio murmurs, tilting her head.

    Agatha unconsciously tongues at her cheek. The imprint of her teeth is roughly cut up inside her mouth, and she presses the tip of her tongue to it with a wince, careful to not bust her lip open again. She watches people walk by the window, the crowd significantly thinner than when she first arrived to Salem.

    “What happened to your lip?” Jen wonders.

    When they first walked up, Agatha was on the wrong side of Jen for the woman to see it. Now, sitting right across from her, it’s likely standing out like a sore thumb. Agatha never looked to see if she now sports a bruise, and she wishes she would have before this moment.

    “Hinge counter door at the clerk’s office,” Agatha lies easily. “I was closer than I thought.” It really isn’t a complete lie. The damn door did clock her across the jaw, but not hard enough to cut her like what she’s wearing now.

    Unconvinced, Jen squints at her and Tilts her chin up. Agatha glances towards William and back to Jen, shaking her head impartially as she sees Jen put two and two together. This time as her eyes skate over the wound, there’s sympathy in them, the kind that knows what those rings might feel like.

    “When do you go back to school, William?” Agatha wonders, watching him blink in surprise at the sudden change in topic.

    “Wednesday,” he sighs. “I don’t want to go back.” William whines childishly and sinks down in his chair. He picks at the cup in front of him, sighing.

    “You’ll get through it, you’re a smart kid,” Agatha says. She offers a stuff closed lip smile as William looks up at her, and he smiles back, sitting up in his chair.

    “How long did you go to school for?” He wonders.

    “Total of nine years, give or take,” she replies, flapping her hand in thought and looking around.

    The coffee shop has slowly emptied out as the morning rush leaves. Agatha hasn’t been paying attention to how long since they came here, and she’s surprised Rio hasn’t made some comment about being on time once again.

    “Why not ten?” Jen asks teasingly. Agatha sticks her tongue out at her.

    “What are you going to do, now, Jen?” Rio chimes, watching her curiously.

    Jen straightens up under Rios attention and narrows her eyes in thought.

    She shrugs and looks down, moving her coffee cup to the side. They all give her time to think and after a while Jen sighs heavily and looks out the window before focusing on Rio again.

    “I’m not. . .entirely sure. If I figure it out, I’ll let you know,” she admits. Jen lifts her hands from her coffee cup and lowers them, her shoulders drooping slightly. Sympathy pangs around in Agatha’s chest. She’d hate to have to stay at the damned mansion with the very woman that caused her downfall.

    “You don’t have Evanora stopping you, anymore,” Rio says, and she and Jen both tense, “you could open your business again.”

    “No, thanks,” Jen laughs, the emptiness of the self-deprecating sound makes William shift uncomfortably. “When I’m able to, I’m going far away from this place.” She looks around at the town on the other side of the glass, her eyes growing distant.

    “In this day and age when in doubt find something on the internet,” Agatha says flatly. “Tons of people do stupid stuff and still make good money.”

    “Thanks,” Jen says flatly, glancing sideways at her.

    “I’m just saying!” Agatha says defensively, beginning to smile. “Sell your used socks if you want.”

    “Can you not be disgusting for five minutes?”

    “Agatha, really?”

    “My roommate did that once!”

    All three of them turn to William in mixed states of shock, curiosity, and disgust. Agatha leans forward, but before she can ask another question that will surely make everyone uncomfortable, Rio stands up.

    “Let’s get going, I want to be in the interstate before noon traffic,” Rio says.

    “Its Sunday,” Agatha points out.

    “Exactly. Church,” Rio replies flatly. She fixes her coat and walks away, taking her empty cup with her.

    Agatha and Jen share a smile about her impatience and then they all follow suit. One by one they file out of the coffee shop and hover on the sidewalk. They’ve never really been huggers, but Jen walks up to her and hugs her anyway. After a moment of her arms hanging uselessly, Agatha hugs her back.

    “I’m glad you came this year,” Jen murmurs. “Maybe don’t avoid me for another decade, again.”

    “No promises,” Agatha mumbles.

    “I mean it, Agatha, I missed you,” Jen says, quite than before.

    When Jen pulls away there are tears threatening the backs of Agatha’s eyes and she mutters to herself as she looks away and tries to disguise the way she wipes at her eyes by tossing her hair aside after. William comes up to her as Jen starts talking quietly to Rio and Agatha smiles at him, and he is instantly attached to her, hugging her tightly.

    “Don’t be a stranger! I’m going to college in NYC, I already texted your phone, so we have each other’s numbers,” he says excitedly.

    Agatha tenses and wants to pull away, but after a moment of thought she sighs and hugs him back. Patting him on the back, Agatha feels the affection she has for the kid warm her from the cold.

    “Call if you need anything, kiddo,” Agatha says quietly. William squeezes her and then pulls away and Agatha pins him with a warning look. “Only for emergencies.”

    William smiles as if he knows she’s putting on a front and he nods endearingly. He and Jen huddle on the sidewalk and they all bid their final goodbyes, Rio falling into step beside her down the sidewalk. Agatha sighs and steps into Rio’s space, wrapping around her arm. She doesn’t miss the gentle smile quirking at Rios lips.

    “I think this is the first time I’ve ever been. . .sad to leave this place,” Agatha admits.

    “Don’t go showing emotion, now, Agatha,” Rio teases flatly, “people will get ideas.” She’s smiling wider as Agatha scoffs and then pulls away from her.

    “Oh, god, no, not me,” she denied with an equally bright smile.

    It is entirely too bittersweet to be driving out of the town, and Agatha stares at the side mirror until it’s faded into the background.

    Chapter End Notes I love writing their sibling arguments lol. It might be one of my favorite things, but you guys already know how much I love banter.
